As for banks and money houses, he only knew that they were places where "fares" came from, and if the police didn't stop rickshaws from parking there, he would definitely get "business." As for what they did inside, he couldn't guess. True, there must be a lot of money here; but why did people specifically come here to "play" [2] with money, he didn't understand; he himself wouldn't easily have anything to do with them anyway, so he didn't bother to worry about it. There were many, many things in the city he didn't understand, and listening to his friends discuss them in tea houses made him even more confused, because everyone had a different opinion, and none of them were thorough. He didn't want to listen anymore, nor did he want to think too much, he knew that if he were to rob, the best place to rob would be a bank; since he didn't want to be a bandit, then he would just keep his own money and not worry about anything else. He thought this was the most experienced approach.

Gao Ma knew he was eager to buy a rickshaw, so she gave him another idea: "Xiangzi, I know you're unwilling to lend money, so you can buy your own rickshaw sooner, that's also an idea! If I were a man, and if I also pulled a rickshaw, I would pull my own rickshaw; pulling it myself and singing my own praises, not asking anyone for help! If I could do that, I wouldn't trade it for being a county magistrate! Pulling a rickshaw is hard work, but if I were a man, with a lot of strength, I would stubbornly pull a rickshaw rather than be a policeman; evergreen in winter and summer, always standing on the street,""I only earn that little bit of money in January, no extra cash, no freedom; as soon as I grow a beard, it just gets blown away, there's simply no improvement. I mean, right, if you want to buy a car quickly, I have a good idea for you: start a money club, ten or at most twenty people, two yuan per person a month, and you take the first turn; won't you immediately have forty-odd yuan? You must have some savings anyway, so scrape together what you have and get a car, just go for it! Once you have the car, you can join a 'black' money club, no interest, and it's a respectable thing, it'll definitely suit your needs! If you really want to start a club, I'll join one, no hesitation! How about it?"

This really made Xiangzi's heart beat faster! If he could really gather thirty or forty yuan, plus the thirty-odd yuan in Old Man Liu's hands, and the few yuan he had now, wouldn't that be around eighty? Although it wouldn't be enough for a brand new car, an 80% new one would certainly be possible! Moreover, this way, he could go to Old Man Liu and get his money back, instead of leaving it there, which felt wrong. 80% new it is, he'd pull it for now, and replace it when he had more to spare.

But where would he find twenty such people? Even if he could gather them, it was a matter of face; if he needed money so badly that he started a club, what if others asked him to join theirs later? Starting a money club, in these hard times, often ended in collapse! A good man doesn't ask for favors; simply put, if he's destined to buy a car, he'll buy it; no asking for favors!

Seeing Xiangzi's lack of reaction, Gao Ma really wanted to tease him, but then she thought of his sincerity and felt a bit embarrassed: "You're really something! 'Chasing a pig down a small alley – straight ahead'; that's good too!"

Xiangzi said nothing. After Gao Ma left, he nodded to himself, as if acknowledging that his stubbornness was admirable, and he felt quite happy.

It was already early winter. In the evenings, besides hawkers selling sugar-roasted chestnuts and peanuts, the low, mournful cry of "chamber pot!" was added to the alley. The chamber pot vendor carried a large, lidded earthenware pot on his pole. Xiangzi bought a large one. It was the first sale, and the vendor couldn't make change. Xiangzi's heart suddenly stirred, and he found the smallest little green chamber pot very interesting, glistening green, with a little pouting spout. "No need for change, I'll take this one!" Putting down the large pot, he carried the small green chamber pot inside: "Is the young master not asleep yet? I've brought you a nice toy!"

Everyone was watching Xiao Wen—the Cao family's little boy—take a bath, and when they saw this item, they couldn't help but laugh. Mr. and Mrs. Cao said nothing, probably feeling that although the item was a bit silly, Xiangzi's good intentions should be appreciated, so they both smiled at him in thanks. Gao Ma's mouth, however, couldn't stay idle: "Look, really, Xiangzi! Such a big fellow, coming up with such a brilliant idea; how unseemly!"

Xiao Wen loved the item and immediately used his hands to scoop water from the bathtub into the small pot: "This little teapot has a big spout!"

Everyone laughed even harder. Xiangzi straightened himself—because he didn't know what to do when he was proud—and walked out. He was very happy; this was something he had never experienced before. Everyone's smiles were directed at him, as if he were a very important person. Smiling, he again took out those few silver dollars, gently placing them one by one into the lidded earthenware pot, thinking to himself: This is more reliable than anything! When the amount is enough, he'll just smash it against the wall; *pacha*, and there'll be more silver dollars than pottery shards!

He decided not to ask anyone for help anymore. Even Old Man Liu, as reliable as he was, sometimes seemed awkward. The money wouldn't be lost in Old Man Liu's hands, but there was always a slight unease. Money was like a ring; it was always best to have it on one's own hand. This decision made him feel relieved, as if his belt had tightened another notch, allowing his chest to stand straighter and firmer.

The weather was getting colder and colder, but Xiangzi didn't seem to notice. With a firm plan in his heart, his vision became brighter; in the light, he wouldn't feel the cold. Ice began to appear on the ground, and even the dirt on the sidewalks solidified. Everywhere showed dryness and firmness, and the color of the black earth had turned slightly yellow, as if all its moisture had evaporated. Especially early in the morning, the ridges of dirt left by large carts were edged with frost, and a sharp little wind scattered the morning glow, revealing an extremely high, blue, and refreshing sky. Xiangzi liked to pull his rickshaw for a run early, the cool wind whistling into his sleeves, making him shiver and feel invigorated as if taking a cold bath. Sometimes a strong wind would come, making it hard for him to breathe, but he would lower his head, grit his teeth, and push forward, like a large fish swimming against the current; the stronger the wind, the greater his resistance, as if he were fighting a decisive battle with the gale. Suddenly, a gust of wind would hit him so hard he couldn't breathe, he'd close his mouth, and after a while, let out a burp, as if he had dived into water. After letting out that burp, he would continue to run forward, pushing ahead, nothing able to stop this giant; every muscle in his body was taut, like a green worm surrounded by ants, shaking its whole body in defense. What a sweat! When he finally put down the rickshaw, straightened his back, let out a long breath, and wiped the yellow sand from the corners of his mouth, he felt invincible; watching the dust-laden wind sweep past him, he nodded. The wind bent the trees by the roadside, tore the shop banners, stripped the posters from the walls, obscured the sun, singing, shouting, roaring, echoing! Suddenly it would rush straight, like a frantic spirit, tearing across the sky and earth; suddenly it would swirl chaotically in all directions, like a demon unsure what to do and deciding to crash about; suddenly it would sweep sideways, attacking everything on the ground unawares, twisting branches, lifting roof tiles, breaking power lines; but Xiangzi stood there watching; he had just come out of the wind, and the wind hadn't been able to do anything to him! Victory belonged to Xiangzi! And when he encountered a tailwind, he only needed to hold the handlebars steady, he didn't need to run himself, the wind would push the wheels for him, like a good friend.

Naturally, since he wasn't blind, he must have also seen those old and weak rickshaw pullers. They wore tattered clothes that a small breeze would penetrate and a strong wind would tear apart; their feet were bound with who knows what. At the rickshaw stand, they shivered, their eyes darting like thieves, and no matter who emerged from anywhere, they would all eagerly ask: "Rickshaw?!" Once they got a fare, they would warm up, their thin, torn clothes soaked with sweat. As soon as they stopped, their sweat would freeze on their backs. When faced with wind, they couldn't lift a single foot, yet they had to drag the rickshaw; if the wind came from above, they had to lower their heads to their chests; if the wind came from below, their feet would lose their footing; if the wind came from the front, a raised hand would send them flying like a kite; if the wind came from behind, they couldn't control the rickshaw or themselves. But they used every method, every ounce of strength, to drag the rickshaw to its destination, risking their lives for a few coppers. After one trip, the dust mixed with sweat into mud, plastered on their faces, leaving only three frozen red circles for their eyes and mouth. The days were so short, so cold, and there weren't many people on the streets; even after such a hard day's work, they might not earn enough for a full meal; but the old ones had wives and children at home; the young ones had parents and siblings! In winter, they were entirely in hell, with one more breath of life than ghosts, but without the leisure and ease of ghosts; ghosts didn't have as many burdens to eat! Dying like a dog in the street was their greatest peace and ease; frozen ghosts, it is said, have a smile on their faces!

How could Xiangzi not have seen all this? But he didn't have time to worry or think about them. Their suffering was also his, but he was young and strong, able to endure hardship, unafraid of cold, unafraid of wind; he had a clean place to stay at night and neat clothes during the day, so he felt that he couldn't be compared to them. Although he was suffering with them now, the degree of suffering was not entirely the same; now he suffered less, and in the future, he could escape from here; he thought that if he reached old age, he would certainly not still be pulling a broken rickshaw and suffering from hunger and cold. He believed that his current advantages could guarantee future victory. Just as those who drove cars wouldn't chat with rickshaw pullers when they met outside restaurants or mansions; car drivers felt it was beneath their dignity to have any dealings with rickshaw pullers. The attitude of car drivers towards rickshaw pullers was somewhat like Xiangzi's attitude towards those old and weak soldiers; they were all in hell, but on different levels. They didn't think they should stand together, but rather each went their own way, individual hopes and efforts blinding each person's eyes, everyone feeling that they could make a living and establish a family with their bare hands, each groping their own way in the darkness. Xiangzi didn't think about others, didn't care about others, he only thought about his own money and future success.

The streets gradually took on a festive atmosphere as the New Year approached. On clear, windless days, although the weather was dry and cold, the roadside was filled with more colors: New Year pictures, gauze lanterns, red plain candles, silk hair ornaments, large and small honey offerings, all displayed, making people feel happy, yet also a little uneasy; because everyone thought of a few days of joy for the New Year, but also had some difficulties, big or small. Xiangzi's eyes brightened. Seeing the New Year goods by the roadside, he thought that the Cao family would surely give gifts; a gift would always mean a few dimes for his wine money. The fixed New Year bonus was two yuan, not much; but when New Year callers came, if he went to see them off, he would get two or three dimes each time. Put together, it would be a sum; he wasn't afraid of small amounts, as long as bits and pieces came in; his earthenware pot wouldn't be unfair to him! In the evenings when he had nothing to do, he would stare at this pottery friend who only ate money and wouldn't spit it out, softly advising: "Eat a lot, eat a lot, buddy! When you've eaten enough, I'll be set!"

The New Year was getting closer and closer; in a flash, it was already the eighth day of the twelfth lunar month. Joy or apprehension forced people to plan and arrange; it was still twenty-four hours a day, but these days were different from usual, they didn't allow anyone to pass them idly, something had to be done, and everything had to be directed towards the New Year, as if time had suddenly gained consciousness and emotion, making people think and busy themselves with it. Xiangzi was on the happy side; the bustling streets, the hawkers' cries, the hope of New Year bonuses and loose change, the New Year's rest, the imagination of good food... all made him happy and expectant like a child. He thought, he should break out a yuan or eighty cents and buy a gift for Old Man Liu. The gift might be small, but the sentiment was great; he had to take something with him, firstly to apologize, as he hadn't been able to visit the old man these days because the mansion was very busy; secondly, he could take the opportunity to ask for those thirty-odd yuan back. Spending a yuan or so to get that sum back was a worthwhile thing. Having decided this, he gently shook the piggy bank, imagining how heavy and pleasant it would sound with another thirty-odd yuan added. Yes, as long as he got that money back, he would have no worries!

One evening, just as he was about to shake that "treasure basin" again, Gao Ma called out to him: "Xiangzi! There's a young lady at the door looking for you; I was just coming back from the street, and she kept asking me about you." When Xiangzi came out, she quietly added: "She looks like a big black tower! Quite frightening!"

Xiangzi's face suddenly turned red as if wrapped in a ball of fire; he knew things were going to go bad!

[1]

"Letting the hawk go" means losing everything.

[2]

"Gudou" means to repeatedly tease or provoke.

Nine

Xiangzi almost didn't have the strength to step over the threshold. Dazed and confused, his feet still inside the doorway, he already saw Miss Liu by the light of the streetlamp. Her face was probably powdered again, and the light made it appear somewhat grayish-green, like a layer of frost on withered black leaves. Xiangzi dared not look her directly in the eye.

Huniu's expression was very complex: her eyes held a longing to see him; but her mouth was slightly open, revealing a hint of a sneer; her nose wrinkled, folding in disdain and eagerness; her eyebrows were furrowed, appearing seductive and domineering on her strangely powdered face. Seeing Xiangzi come out, her lips pursed a few times, and the various expressions on her face momentarily found no suitable place to settle, staring blankly at the ground. She swallowed some saliva, seemingly suppressing the complex emotions and feelings, and put on a bit of the worldly air she had picked up from Old Man Liu, half annoyed, half smiling, pretending not to care much as she let out a laugh: "You're really something! Like a meat bun thrown to a dog, once it's gone, it never comes back!" Her voice was loud, just as it was when she argued with the rickshaw pullers at the rickshaw stand. After saying these two sentences, the smile on her face completely vanished, and she suddenly seemed to feel a sense of shame and degradation, biting her lip.

"Don't shout!" Xiangzi seemed to put all his strength into his lips, bursting out these two words, very softly, but with immense power.

"Hmph! I'm not afraid!" She smiled maliciously, but as if against her will, she lowered her voice slightly. "No wonder you're hiding from me, so there's a little vixen-like maid here; I've known for a long time that you're no good, don't look so big, dumb, and crude, like a Tartar pulling out his pipe, pretending to be dumb when he's not!" Her voice rose again. "Don't shout!" Xiangzi was only afraid that Gao Ma was secretly listening at the door. "Don't shout! Come over here!" He said, walking towards the road.

"I'm not afraid no matter where I go, I just have a loud voice!" She retorted, but she followed him.

Crossing the road, they came to the eastern sidewalk, close to the red wall of the park. Xiangzi—still not forgetting his country habits—squatted down. "What are you doing here?" "Me? Hmph, I have a lot of things to do!" She put her left hand on her hip,"""His stomach protruded a bit. He looked down at her, thought for a moment, and as if a spark of kindness had touched him, pitying him, she said, "Xiangzi! I need to talk to you, something important!"

That soft "Xiangzi" dissipated much of his anger. He looked up at her; she still had nothing endearing about her, but that "Xiangzi" still echoed faintly in his heart, carrying a gentle intimacy, as if he had heard it somewhere before, evoking an undeniable, hard-to-break affection. He still spoke in a low voice, but it was softer now: "What is it?"

"Xiangzi!" She leaned closer, "I'm pregnant!"

"Pregnant with what?" He was momentarily stunned.

"This!" She pointed to her stomach. "You figure it out!"

Dumbfounded, he gasped, and suddenly understood everything. A myriad of things he had never thought of rushed into his mind, so many, so urgent, so chaotic, that his mind abruptly became a blank, like a film reel suddenly breaking. The street was very quiet, some gray clouds covered the moon, and from time to time, a small breeze blew, rustling withered branches and leaves. In the distance, there were a few sharp catcalls. Xiangzi's mind went from chaotic to blank, not even hearing these sounds; he propped his chin in his hand, staring blankly at the ground, making the ground seem to move; he couldn't think of anything, nor did he want to think of anything; he only felt himself getting smaller and smaller, yet unable to completely shrink into the earth. His entire life seemed to rest on this discomfort; nothing else! Only then did he feel the cold, even his lips trembled slightly.

"Don't just squat there, speak up! Get up!" She also seemed to feel the cold and wanted to move a few steps.

He stood up stiffly, unwillingly, and followed her northward, still unable to find anything to say. His whole body felt numb, as if he had just woken up from being frozen.

"You have no ideas?" She glanced at Xiangzi, a look of pity in her eyes.

He had nothing to say.

"On the twenty-seventh, Dad's birthday, you must come." "Busy, it's the end of the year!" Xiangzi, in his extremely chaotic mind, hadn't forgotten his own affairs. "I know you're a tough nut to crack, so talking nicely to you is a waste of time!" Her voice rose again, the quietness of the street made her voice sound particularly clear, making Xiangzi particularly uncomfortable. "Do you think I'm afraid of anyone? What do you plan to do? If you don't want to listen to me, I don't have time to waste my breath on you! If things go sour, I'll block your gate and curse for three days and three nights! Wherever you go, I'll find you! I don't care about the consequences!"

"Can you stop yelling?" Xiangzi stepped away from her.

"Afraid of yelling, then don't be greedy in the first place! You've had your fun, leaving me to take the blame alone, and you don't even open your dead eyes to see who I am!"

"Speak slowly, I'm listening!" Xiangzi had originally felt very cold, but this scolding suddenly made him feel hot. The heat wanted to break through his frozen skin, his whole body felt a bit itchy, and his scalp was particularly irritated.

"That's settled then! Don't look for trouble!" She pouted, revealing two canine teeth. "Don't be ungrateful, I truly care for you, so don't be unappreciative! If you cross me, it won't end well for you, I'm telling you!"

"No..." Xiangzi wanted to say, "No need to hit me and then rub it," but he hadn't thought it through completely; he knew many Beijing witticisms, but he couldn't articulate them well; when others spoke them, he understood, but he couldn't say them himself.

"No what?"

"Speak your mind!"

"I have a good idea for you," Tigress stood still, facing him, "Look, if you were to ask a matchmaker to speak for you, Dad would definitely not agree. He's a cart owner, you're a rickshaw puller, he won't stoop to having relatives like that. I don't care, I like you, and isn't that enough? What else matters! No one else can be my matchmaker, as soon as someone proposes, Dad will think they're after his dozens of carts; even people of higher status than you won't do. This matter I must handle myself, and I've chosen you. We'll act first and report later; anyway, I'm already pregnant, neither of us can run away! But, if we just go straight to him and tell him, it still won't work. The older Dad gets, the more muddled he becomes. If we let slip a word, he'll go marry a young concubine and kick me out. Dad is strong, don't let his nearly seventy years fool you. If he really marries a young concubine, I can't say how many, but I guarantee he can still produce two or three children, believe it or not!" "Let's talk while we walk." Xiangzi saw the patrolling policeman had walked by twice, feeling uneasy.

"Let's talk right here, who can stop us!" She followed Xiangzi's gaze and also saw the policeman: "You're not pulling a rickshaw, why are you afraid of him? Can he just bite off someone's private parts for no reason? That would be truly wicked! We'll talk about our business! Look, this is what I'm thinking: on the twenty-seventh, Dad's birthday, you go kowtow to him three times. Then, after the New Year, you go again to pay your respects and win his favor. I think once he's happy, get some wine or something, and let him drink his fill. When he's about seven or eight parts drunk, strike while the iron's hot, and you simply acknowledge him as your godfather. Later, I'll slowly let him know that I'm pregnant. He'll surely question me, and I'll give him the 'Xu Shu entering Cao Cao's camp - not a word spoken' treatment. Only when he's truly anxious will I reveal someone, saying it's Qiao Er, who recently died - the second manager of our eastern coffin shop. He has no relatives or friends, and is already buried in the public cemetery outside Dongzhimen. Where will Dad go to investigate? When Dad is at a loss, we'll slowly persuade him, it would be best if he gave me to you. You're already his godson, and then his son-in-law, it's pretty much the same; it's like pushing a boat with the current, saving everyone from embarrassment. Don't you think my idea is good?"

Xiangzi said nothing.

Feeling that she had made her point, Tigress began to walk north, her head bowed, as if admiring her own words, and also as if giving Xiangzi a chance to ponder. At this moment, the wind tore open a patch of gray clouds, revealing moonlight. The two had reached the north end of the street. The water of the Imperial River had long been frozen solid, quiet, gray-bright, flat, and firm, supporting the walls of the Forbidden City. There was no sound within the Forbidden City, the exquisite corner towers, the golden and green archways, the vermilion city gates, and the pavilions on Jingshan, all silently seemed to be listening to sounds that were hard to hear again. A small breeze blew, like a sigh, gently passing through the towers and palaces, as if to reveal a message from history. Tigress walked west, and Xiangzi followed to Jin'ao Yu. There were almost no pedestrians on the bridge, the faint moonlight coldly illuminated the two large ice rinks on either side of the bridge. In the distance, the pavilions were dim with some dark shadows, quietly frozen on the lake, only the yellow tiles on top glimmered faintly. The trees swayed slightly, and the moonlight appeared even more hazy; the White Pagoda, however, towered into the clouds, foolishly white, making everything cold and desolate. The entire Three Seas, in its artificial carving, revealed the bleakness of the northern land. At the head of the bridge, the cold air from the ice on both sides made Xiangzi shiver. He didn't want to walk any further. Normally, when he pulled his rickshaw across the bridge, his attention was entirely on his feet, fearing a mistake, and he couldn't spare a glance to either side. Now, he could look freely, but he felt this scenery was somewhat terrifying: those gray, cold ice, the slightly swaying tree shadows, the pale white pagoda, all seemed so lonely that they might suddenly scream or run wildly! Even this large white stone bridge beneath his feet appeared unusually empty and particularly white, even the lamplight was a bit desolate. He didn't want to walk any further, didn't want to look anymore, and even less did he want to accompany her; he truly wanted to jump down at once, headfirst, smash through the ice, and sink, frozen in the ice like a dead fish.

"See you tomorrow!" He suddenly turned and walked back.

"Xiangzi! That's how it's going to be, see you on the twenty-seventh!" She said to Xiangzi's broad, straight back. After speaking, she glanced at the White Pagoda, sighed, and walked west. Xiangzi didn't even turn his head, as if a ghost was following him, and in a few strides, he reached Tuancheng, walking so hastily that he almost bumped into the city wall. Leaning against the wall with one hand, he couldn't help but want to cry. After a moment of愣, a voice called from the bridge: "Xiangzi! Xiangzi! Come here! Xiangzi!" It was Tigress's voice!

He slowly shuffled two steps towards the bridge. Tigress was walking down, her head tilted slightly, her mouth slightly open: "I said Xiangzi, come here; for you!" Before he had moved many steps, she was already in front of him: "Here, your thirty-odd yuan; there are a few dimes in change, I made it up to a whole yuan for you. Here! Not for anything else, just to show my heart, I care about you, I love you, I protect you! Don't say anything else, just don't be ungrateful! Here! Hold it tight, don't blame me if you lose it!"

Xiangzi took the money—a wad of banknotes—and after a moment of stunned silence, he couldn't find anything to say.

"Alright, we'll see each other on the twenty-seventh! Don't leave without me!" She smiled. "The advantage is yours, you can calculate it carefully yourself!" She turned and walked back.

He clutched the wad of bills, staring blankly at her until the arch of the bridge obscured her head. The gray clouds again covered the moonlight; the lights grew brighter, the bridge was exceptionally white, empty, and cold. He turned, quickened his pace, and walked back like a madman; when he reached the street gate, the pale, desolate image of the bridge still lingered in his mind, as if only an instant had passed.

Back in his room, he first counted the banknotes; he counted them two or three times, the sweat on his palms making the bills sticky, and he couldn't get a clear count. After counting, he put them in the money jar. Sitting on the edge of the bed, staring blankly at the earthenware pot, he decided not to think about anything; with money, there was a way. He firmly believed that this piggy bank would solve everything for him, and he didn't need to think anymore. The Imperial River, Jingshan, the White Pagoda, the big bridge, Tigress, the belly... all were dreams; when he woke up from the dream, there were more than thirty yuan in the piggy bank, real money!

Having looked enough, he hid the piggy bank, intending to sleep soundly. No matter how great the difficulty, he could sleep through it and deal with it tomorrow!

Lying down, he couldn't close his eyes! Those things were like a swarm of bees, one coming out, another going in, each with a sting at its tip!

He didn't want to think, and indeed, there was no way to think. Tigress had blocked all paths, and he couldn't escape.

The best thing would be to stomp his foot and leave. Xiangzi couldn't leave. Even if he were asked to guard the White Pagoda in Beihai, he would be willing; he just couldn't go to the countryside! Go to another city? He couldn't think of a better place than Beiping. He couldn't leave, he wanted to die here.

Since he didn't want to leave, there was no need to waste energy thinking about other things. What Tigress said, she would do; if he didn't follow her path, she would truly keep bothering him; as long as he was in Beiping, she would find him! With her, he had to be serious, no need to try to be clever. If he angered her, she would even bring up Liu Siye, and if Liu Siye sold one or two people—not to mention more—in some secluded place, he could also take Xiangzi's life!

He thought about Tigress's words from beginning to end, and he felt like he had fallen into a trap, his hands and feet caught in clamps, with no way to escape. He couldn't criticize her ideas one by one, so he couldn't find any flaws in them. He only felt that she had cast a net that left no escape, not even a small fish an inch long could get out! Since he couldn't think about each detail, he made it all into a whole, like a thousand-pound gate pressing down, all pressing on his head. Under this irresistible pressure, he realized that a rickshaw puller's lifelong fate was encapsulated in two words—bad luck! A rickshaw puller, being a rickshaw puller, should do nothing, not even get involved with women; getting involved would lead to a huge mistake. Liu Siye relied on dozens of rickshaws, and Tigress would rely on a stinking thing to bully him! He didn't need to think deeply about anything; if he decided to accept his fate, then fine, go kowtow and acknowledge a godfather, and then wait to marry that stinking monster. If he didn't accept his fate, he would have to risk his life!

Thinking of this, he put Tigress and her words aside; no, this wasn't her power, but the fate of a rickshaw puller, just like a dog is bound to be beaten and bullied, even children would hit it with two sticks for no reason. What was the point of such a life? If he had to risk it, then so be it!

He didn't sleep. He kicked off the quilt and sat up. He decided to get some wine and get completely drunk; what was a problem, what was a rule, damn it all! Get drunk, sleep! The twenty-seventh? He wouldn't go kowtow even on the twenty-eighth, let's see what anyone could do to Xiangzi!

Donning his thick cotton coat and holding the small rice bowl he used as a teacup, he ran out. The wind grew stronger, the gray clouds in the sky had dispersed, and the moon was small, casting a cold light. Xiangzi had just come out of his warm bed, constantly sucking in air. The street was practically empty of pedestrians, and there were only one or two rickshaws by the roadside, their pullers warming their hands over their ears, stomping their feet beside the rickshaws. Xiangzi ran all the way to the small shop in the south. To preserve the warmth, the shop had already closed its main door, collecting money and handing out goods through a small window. Xiangzi ordered four liang of bai gan (white liquor) and three large copper coins' worth of peanuts. Holding the wine bowl level, he dared not run, but walked quickly like a sedan chair carrier, returning to his room. He quickly burrowed into the quilt, his upper and lower teeth chattering for a while, unwilling to sit up again. The wine on the table emitted a pungent odor, which he didn't particularly like, and he seemed to have no interest in the peanuts either. This sudden chill seemed like a basin of cold water waking him up. His hands were too lazy to reach out, and his heart was no longer so warm. He lay there for a long time,"""His eyes, from the edge of the quilt, looked again at the wine bowl on the table. No, he couldn't ruin himself for that entanglement, couldn't break his vow of abstinence from then on. Things were indeed difficult, but there was always a way for him to get through. Even if there was no escape at all, he shouldn't roll into the mud pit himself first; he had to keep his eyes open, clearly watching how others pushed him down in the end.

He put out the lamp, covered his head completely with the quilt, and thought he would just fall asleep like that. Still unable to sleep, he lifted the quilt to look, and the window paper was tinged blue by the moonlight in the courtyard, as if dawn was breaking. His nose felt the cold in the room, and the cold air carried a hint of wine. He suddenly sat up, fumbled for the wine bowl, and took a big gulp!

Individual solutions, Xiangzi wasn't that clever. A complete reckoning, he didn't have the courage for that. So, with no solution at all, he circled around all day, full of grievances. Just like all living things, after being harmed, he could only helplessly try to pick up the pieces himself. The cricket with its broken leg still tried to crawl with its tiny legs. Xiangzi had no definite plan, only wanted to slowly get through day by day, one thing after another, crawling as far as he could, not even thinking of jumping up.

There were still more than ten days until the twenty-seventh, and he was completely focused on that day. What he thought, what he muttered, what he dreamed of, was all the twenty-seventh. It was as if once the twenty-seventh passed, he would have a solution for everything, even though he knew he was deceiving himself. Sometimes he would think further ahead, for example, taking the dozens of yuan he had to Tianjin; once there, he might coincidentally change his profession and no longer pull a rickshaw. Could Huniu still chase him to Tianjin? In his mind, any place reached by train must be very far, and no matter what, she couldn't chase him there. He thought it was a good idea, but his conscience knew this was only a last resort; if he could stay in Beiping, he would stay in Beiping! This made him think of the twenty-seventh again, still thinking of the convenient and easy way, hoping that if he could just get through this hurdle, he might be able to resolve the matter without changing the overall situation; even if he couldn't completely get rid of everything, at least getting through one hurdle was better than nothing.

How to get through this hurdle? He had two ideas: one was to ignore her completely and simply not attend the birthday celebration. The other was to do as she instructed. Although these two ideas were different, the result was the same: if he didn't go, she certainly wouldn't let it go; if he went, she wouldn't spare him either. He still remembered when he first started pulling a rickshaw, imitating others, he would duck into any alley to take a shortcut, only to mistakenly enter a circular alley; he went in a circle and ended up back on the original street. Now he had entered such a small alley again, as if no matter which way he went, the result would be the same.

In his helplessness, he tried to think positively, what if he just took her, what was so wrong with that? But no matter how he thought about it, he felt stifled. Thinking of her appearance, he could only shake his head. Never mind her appearance, think of her behavior; hmph! With his strong will and proper conduct, to marry such a broken woman, he wouldn't be able to face anyone, not even his parents after death! Who knew if the child in her belly was his or not? True, she would bring a few rickshaws; could he be sure? Old Man Liu wasn't someone to be trifled with! Even if everything went smoothly, he couldn't stand it, could he outdo Huniu? She only needed to extend a little finger to make him dizzy and disoriented, unable to tell north from south. He knew how formidable she was! To start a family, he absolutely couldn't have her, there was nothing else to say! If he took her, he would be gone, and he wasn't someone who looked down on himself! No way!

Unable to deal with her, he turned to hating himself, wanting to slap himself a few times. But, truthfully, he hadn't done anything wrong. Everything was arranged by her, just waiting for him to fall into the trap. The problem seemed to be that he was too honest, and honesty inevitably led to suffering, with no reason to be found!

What made him even sadder was having nowhere to vent his grievances. He had no parents, no siblings, no friends. In the past, he felt like a hero, with the sky above his head and the earth beneath his feet, free from worries. Now, he finally understood, finally realized, that people cannot live alone. Especially those in the same profession, they all seemed a bit lovable now. If he had made a few friends in the past, he thought, big men like himself, even with another Huniu, he wouldn't be afraid; they would give him advice, they would help him fight and exert themselves. But he was always alone; it wasn't easy to find friends at the last minute! He felt a fear he had never experienced before. If things continued like this, anyone would bully him; being alone, he couldn't withstand the world!

This fear made him begin to doubt himself. In winter, when his master had a dinner party or went to the opera, he would, as usual, tuck the carbide lamp's water bottle into his bosom; because if he left it on the rickshaw, it would freeze. After running, covered in sweat, he would press that icy cold small water bottle against his chest, making him shiver instantly; it would take a while for that water bottle to warm up a bit. But normally, he didn't think there was anything unreasonable about this; sometimes, carrying it, he even felt it was a kind of superiority, as those pulling broken rickshaws couldn't even use carbide lamps. Now, he seemed to realize that he only earned so much money a month, and had to endure all the hardships, not even allowing a small water bottle to freeze, but having to hold it against his chest. How wide his chest was, yet it seemed less valuable than a small bottle. Originally, he thought pulling a rickshaw was his ideal job, and through it, he could start a family and career. Now, he secretly shook his head. No wonder Huniu bullied him, he was originally just a person not even as good as a small water bottle!

On the third night after Huniu came looking for him, Mr. Cao and his friends went to see a late-night movie. Xiangzi was waiting in a small teahouse, with the small bottle, like a piece of ice, tucked into his chest. The weather was extremely cold, the doors and windows of the small teahouse were tightly shut, filled with coal gas, sweat, and the dry smoke of cheap cigarettes. Even so, there was a layer of ice flowers on the windows. Most of the tea drinkers were rickshaw pullers hired by the month. Some leaned their heads against the wall, taking advantage of the warmth in the room, closing their eyes and dozing off. Some held bowls of baijiu, offering it to everyone, and then slowly told stories from beginning to end while drinking, taking a sip, smacking their lips, and then loudly exhaling cold air. Some clutched large flatbreads, biting off half a piece at a time, making their necks thick and red. Some had stern faces, complaining generally to everyone about how they hadn't stopped since early morning, their clothes having been wet and dry, dry and wet countless times! Most of the others were chatting amongst themselves. Hearing these two sentences, they all fell silent for a moment, and then, like birds scattering from a nest, they all remembered the day's grievances and wanted to tell everyone. Even the one eating the flatbread managed to create a gap in his mouth to move his tongue, swallowing the bread while talking, the veins on his head throbbing: "You think those of us who pull rickshaws by the month don't suffer, damn it?! I've been working since two o'clock, damn it—hiccup!—and haven't had a bite to eat! They keep saying from Qianmen to Pingzemen—hiccup!—I've pulled three round trips, damn it! In this weather, my ass is frozen and cracked, damn it, constantly letting out gas!" He looked around at everyone, nodded, and bit off another piece of bread.

This turned everyone's conversation back to the weather, and centered on the weather, each expressed their hardships. Xiangzi remained silent throughout, but listened carefully to what they said. Although everyone's words differed in tone, intonation, and facts, they were all curses and grievances. These words, encountering the grievances in his own heart, were like raindrops falling on parched earth, all absorbed. He couldn't, and wouldn't, tell his own story from beginning to end; he could only absorb the bitterness of life from others' words. Everyone was suffering, and he was no exception; he recognized himself and wanted to sympathize with everyone. When they spoke of sorrowful things, he frowned; when they spoke of amusing things, he also pursed his lips. In this way, he felt that he was united with them, they were all suffering friends, and even though he said nothing, it didn't matter much. In the past, he thought they were just poor-mouthed and malicious, that they wouldn't get rich by talking all day long. Today, it seemed like the first time he felt that they weren't just talking idly, but speaking for him, expressing the hardships of him and all rickshaw pullers.

Just as everyone was talking animatedly, the door suddenly opened, and a gust of cold air rushed in. Everyone almost glared outwards, wondering who was so unpopular as to push the door open. The more anxious everyone became, the slower the person outside moved, seemingly deliberately annoying them.

The teahouse waiter, half-anxious and half-smiling, shouted, "Hurry up, my dear uncle! Don't let all the warmth out!"

Before he finished speaking, the person from outside came in, also a rickshaw puller. He looked over fifty years old, wearing a cotton-padded jacket that was neither short enough nor long enough, like a lotus pod, with cotton exposed at the lapels and elbows. His face seemed not to have been washed for many days, showing no flesh color, only his ears were frozen crimson, red as if they were about to fall off. His pale hair was disheveled under a broken small hat; ice beads hung on his eyebrows and short beard. As soon as he entered, he grabbed a bench and sat down, struggling to say, "Brew a pot."

This teahouse had always been a gathering place for rickshaw pullers hired by the month. An old rickshaw puller like this would never come in on a normal day.

Everyone looked at him, all seeming to feel something more profound than what had just been said, and no one wanted to speak again. On a normal day, there would always be one or two less sensible youngsters who would find some witty remarks to tease such a customer, but today no one made a sound.

Before the tea was brewed, the old rickshaw puller's head slowly lowered, and as it lowered, his whole body slid down.

Everyone immediately stood up: "What's wrong? What's wrong?" Saying this, they all wanted to rush forward.

"Don't move!" The teahouse owner, experienced, stopped everyone. He went over alone, unbuttoned the old rickshaw puller's collar, helped him up on the spot, propped a chair behind him, and held his shoulders: "Sugar water, quickly!" After saying that, he listened to the old rickshaw puller's neck and muttered to himself, "It's not phlegm!"

No one moved, but no one sat down either. They all blinked their eyes in the smoky room, looking towards the door. Everyone seemed to think to themselves, "This is our example! When our hair turns pale, anyone can fall and die in this business!"

As the sugar water was brought to the old rickshaw puller's lips, he groaned twice. Still with his eyes closed, he raised his right hand—his hand was shiny black, as if lacquered—and wiped his mouth with the back of his hand.

"Drink some water!" the owner said into his ear.

"Ah?" The old rickshaw puller opened his eyes. He saw himself sitting on the ground, curled his legs, and tried to stand up.

"Drink some water first, no need to rush," the owner said, releasing his hand. Everyone almost ran over.

"Hey! Hey!" The old rickshaw puller looked around, cupped the teacup with both hands, and sipped the sugar water mouthful by mouthful.

Slowly finishing the sugar water, he looked at everyone again: "Hey, thank you all for your trouble!" He spoke very gently and kindly, absolutely unlike something that would come from that scruffy mouth. After speaking, he tried to stand up again, and three or four people rushed to help him up. A slight smile appeared on his face, and he said gently again, "It's fine, it's fine, no problem! I was just cold and hungry, and felt dizzy for a moment! It's nothing!" Although his face was covered in so much dirt, that slight smile made everyone feel as if they saw a kind and clean face.

Everyone seemed to be moved. The middle-aged man holding a bowl of wine had already finished his drink, his eyes bloodshot, and now with some tears: "Come, come, two liang!" When the wine arrived, the old rickshaw puller was already sitting on a chair against the wall. He was a little tipsy, but he respectfully placed the wine in front of the old rickshaw puller: "My treat, please drink! I'm also over forty, to be honest, pulling a rickshaw by the month is just making do, one year at a time, my legs know! In another two or three years, I'll be just like you! You must be almost sixty, right?"

"Still young, fifty-five!" The old rickshaw puller took a sip of wine. "It's cold, no passengers. Me, ah, my stomach is empty; even if I had some money, I'd drink wine to warm up! Walking here, I really couldn't hold on anymore, wanted to come in and get warm. The room was too hot, and I hadn't eaten, so I must have fainted. It's nothing, nothing! Thank you all, brothers!"

At this moment, the old man's straw-like gray hair, the dirt on his face, his charcoal-stick-like hands, and his broken hat and cotton-padded jacket all seemed to emit a pure light, like the statues in a dilapidated temple, broken yet still dignified. Everyone looked at him, as if fearing he would leave. Xiangzi remained silent, standing there blankly. Hearing the old rickshaw puller say his stomach was empty, he suddenly ran out, and then ran back like a flash, holding ten lamb-filled buns on a cabbage leaf. He brought them directly to the old man's eyes and said, "Eat!" Then, he sat back in his original spot, lowered his head, as if very tired. "Ah!" The old man seemed to be happy, and also crying, nodding to everyone. "They are indeed brothers! Pulling a rickshaw, no matter how much effort you put in, it's hard to ask for even one extra coin at the end!" Saying this, he stood up, wanting to leave.

"Eat!" Everyone almost shouted in unison.

"I'll call Xiaoma'er, my grandson, he's watching the rickshaw outside!" "I'll go, you sit down!" the middle-aged rickshaw puller said, "You won't lose the rickshaw here, rest assured, the police station is right across the street." He opened the door a crack: "Xiaoma'er! Xiaoma'er! Your grandpa is calling you! Bring the rickshaw here!"

The old man touched the buns several times but never picked one up. As soon as Xiaoma'er entered the door, he picked one up: "Xiaoma'er, good boy, here you go!" Xiaoma'er was only twelve or thirteen, with a very thin face, but he was dressed warmly, his nose frozen crimson, with two white snot trails.Wearing a pair of broken earmuffs. Standing beside the old man, he took a steamed bun with his right hand, and automatically picked up another with his left, taking a bite out of one.

“Hey! Slowly!” The old man put one hand on his grandson’s head, picked up a steamed bun with the other, and slowly brought it to his mouth. “Grandpa only needs two, the rest are all yours! After you finish eating, we’ll put the cart away and go home, no more pulling. If it’s not so cold tomorrow, we’ll go out earlier. Right, little pony?”

The little pony nodded at the steamed bun, sniffled: “Grandpa, eat three, the rest are mine. I’ll pull Grandpa home later!” “No need!” The old man smiled proudly at everyone: “We’ll still walk later, it’s cold sitting in the cart.”

The old man finished his share, drank the wine in his cup, and waited for the little pony to finish his buns. He pulled out a piece of rag, wiped his mouth, and nodded to everyone again: “My son went to be a soldier, and never came back; my daughter-in-law—”

“Don’t say that!” The little pony’s cheeks were puffed out like two small peaches, as he ate and spoke, trying to stop his grandpa.

“It’s okay to talk! They’re not outsiders!” Then he whispered to everyone: “The child is sensitive, you can’t imagine how strong-willed she is! My daughter-in-law also left. The two of us, grandpa and grandson, live off this cart; the cart is old, but it’s ours, and we don’t have to worry about the cart fee every day. Whether we earn a lot or a little, we two old men just muddle through, there’s nothing we can do! Nothing!”

“Grandpa,” the little pony had almost finished his bun, and tugged at the old man’s sleeve, “we still have to pull one more trip, we don’t even have money to buy coal tomorrow morning! It’s all your fault, just now you refused to pull someone to the back door for twenty coppers, if it were up to me, I would have pulled them, but you just wouldn’t! If there’s no coal tomorrow morning, what will you do!”

“I have a way, Grandpa will go and buy five catties of coal balls on credit.”

“And some kindling?”

“That’s right! Good boy, eat; after you finish, we should get going!” Saying that, the old man stood up, and said to everyone in a circle: “Thank you all, brothers, for your trouble!” He reached out to pull the little pony, who stuffed the unfinished bun whole into his mouth. Some people remained seated, others followed out. Xiangzi was the first to follow out, he wanted to see that cart.

A very broken cart, the paint on the cart board was cracked, the handle was worn down to reveal the wood grain, a rattling broken lamp, and the support poles of the canopy were tied with hemp rope. The little pony found a match in his earmuffs, struck it on the sole of his shoe, and cupping it with his two small black hands, lit the lamp. The old man spat into his palm, sighed, and picked up the cart handles, “See you tomorrow, brothers!”

Xiangzi stood blankly outside the door, watching the old man, the young boy, and the broken cart. The old man kept talking as he walked, his voice rising and falling; the streetlights and shadows on the road flickered on and off. Xiangzi listened and watched, feeling an unprecedented discomfort in his heart. In the little pony, he seemed to see his own past; in the old man, he seemed to see his own future! He had never easily let go of a single coin, but now he felt very happy, having bought ten steamed buns for this old man and young boy. Only when they were out of sight did he re-enter the room. Everyone started laughing and talking again, he felt confused, paid for the tea, and walked out again, pulling his cart to wait for Mr. Cao outside the cinema.

The weather was truly cold. Grey dust floated in the air, the wind seemed to be rushing above, the stars were not very clear, only a few large ones trembled slightly in the sky. There was no wind on the ground, but cold air emanated from all around, there were already several long frozen cracks in the cart tracks, the earth was greyish-white, as cold and hard as ice. Xiangzi stood outside the cinema for a while, already feeling cold, but he didn't want to go back to the teahouse. He wanted to quietly think by himself. That old man and young boy seemed to have shattered his greatest hope—the old man's cart was his own! From the first day he pulled a cart, he had decided to buy his own cart, and now he was still working hard all day for this goal; with his own cart, he thought, he would have everything. Humph, look at that old man!

He refused to marry Huniu, wasn't it because he wanted to buy a cart? Buy a cart, save money, and then marry a wife with a clean slate; humph, look at the little pony! If he had a son, it might not be any different.

Thinking this way, resisting Huniu's demands seemed unnecessary; anyway, he couldn't escape his fate, so what kind of woman couldn't he marry? Besides, she might even bring a few carts with her, why not enjoy a few days of ready-made blessings! Once he understood himself, there was no need to look down on others, Huniu was Huniu, nothing more needed to be said!

The movie ended, and he quickly set up his small water bottle and lit the lamp. He even took off his padded jacket, leaving only a small vest, he wanted to run as fast as he could, to run away from everything, even if he fell and died, it wouldn't matter much!

[1]

磨烦 (mófán), meaning to procrastinate.

[2]

横是 (héngshì), meaning probably.

Eleven

As soon as he thought of the old man and the little pony, Xiangzi wanted to abandon all hope and just live one day at a time. Why bother constantly gritting his teeth and making things difficult for himself?! He seemed to understand the fate of the poor, it was like a jujube pit, pointed at both ends: it was a miracle if you didn't starve to death when you were young; it was very difficult not to starve to death when you were old. Only the middle part, when you were young and strong, not afraid of hunger, fullness, or hard work, could you live like a human being. In this period, if you didn't dare to enjoy yourself when you should, you were a complete fool; once this village was passed, there would be no such shop! Thinking this way, he didn't even want to worry about Huniu anymore.

As soon as he saw that silent jar, his thoughts turned again. No, he couldn't be careless; he was only a few tens of yuan away from buying a cart, he couldn't let all his previous efforts go to waste; at least he couldn't just throw away the little savings in the jar, which he had saved with such difficulty! He still had to follow the right path, definitely! But what about Huniu? There was still no solution, he still had to worry about that hateful twenty-seven.

Worried to the point of helplessness, he hugged the earthenware jar and muttered to himself: Let it be whatever it wants, anyway, this money is mine! No one can snatch it away! With this money, Xiangzi fears nothing! If I get anxious, I'll stomp my foot and run, with money, my legs will move!

The streets became increasingly lively, sugar melons for the Kitchen God festival filled the streets, and everywhere you went you could hear the sound of "buy sugar, buy sugar." Xiangzi had originally looked forward to the New Year, but now he was not at all enthusiastic, the more chaotic the streets, the tighter his heart became, that terrible twenty-seven was right before his eyes! His eyes sank, and even the scar on his face darkened a little. Pulling the cart, the streets were so chaotic, the ground so slippery, he had to be extra careful. Worries and caution attacked him from both sides, he felt his energy was insufficient, thinking of one thing and forgetting another, often suddenly startled, his body itching as if a child had prickly heat in summer.

On the afternoon of the Kitchen God festival, a gentle east wind brought a day of dark clouds. The weather suddenly warmed up a bit. As it approached dusk, the wind became even lighter, and sparse snowflakes fell from the sky. The sugar melon vendors all became anxious, with the warm weather and snowflakes, everyone frantically sprinkled white powder on the sugar, still afraid that it would all stick together. Not many snowflakes fell before they turned into small pellets, making a soft swishing sound, covering the ground in white. After seven o'clock, shops and households began to worship the Kitchen God, amidst the incense and firecrackers, dense small snow fell, adding a somewhat eerie atmosphere to the festivity. People on the street all looked a bit anxious, pedestrians and those in carts were all eager to go home to worship the gods, but the ground was wet and slippery, so they dared not walk quickly. The sugar vendors were eager to sell their seasonal goods, shouting breathlessly, which sounded quite unsettling.

It was probably nine o'clock, and Xiangzi was pulling Mr. Cao home from the West City. After passing the lively section of Xidan Archway, they entered Chang'an Street to the east, and the crowd gradually thinned out. A thin layer of snow covered the flat asphalt road, glistening slightly under the streetlights. Occasionally, a car would pass by, its headlights shining far, and the small snow pellets in the light had a yellowish glow, like countless golden sands being sprinkled. Nearing the Xinhua Gate area, the road was originally very wide, and with the thin snow, it made one feel even more expansive and refreshed, and everything seemed more solemn. The "Chang'an Archway," the gate tower of Xinhua Gate, and the red walls of Nanhai, all wore white crowns, complementing the vermilion pillars and red walls, quietly displaying the dignity of the ancient capital under the lamplight. At this time and place, one felt as if Beiping had no residents, but was simply a jade palace in the sky, with only some old pines silently receiving the snowflakes. Xiangzi had no time to admire these beautiful sights, looking at the "jade road" before him, he only wanted to reach home in one step; that straight, white, cold road seemed to lead his mind's eye directly to his doorstep. But he couldn't run fast, although the snow on the ground wasn't thick, it would quickly stick to the soles of his shoes in a thick layer; if he stomped it off, it would stick again in a moment. The sleet was very small, but heavy and substantial, both hindering his feet and blurring his vision, he couldn't run quickly. The snow pellets hitting his body didn't melt easily, a thin layer had already accumulated on his shoulders, though it wasn't much, the dampness made him feel uncomfortable. There were no shops in this area, but the distant sound of firecrackers continued incessantly, occasionally shooting up a double bang or a "five ghosts making trouble for the judge" into the dark sky. Sparks scattered, making the sky appear even darker, almost terrifyingly so. Listening to the firecrackers, seeing the sparks and darkness in the sky, he wanted to go home immediately. But he dared not let his legs loose, it was awkward!

What made him even more unhappy was that from West City, he felt a bicycle following him. When they reached West Chang'an Street, the street became quieter, and he felt the pursuit behind him even more clearly—the bicycle wheels crunched on the thin snow, and although the sound wasn't loud, he could feel it. Xiangzi, like other rickshaw pullers, hated bicycles the most. Cars were annoying, but their sound was loud, so he could avoid them from afar. Bicycles would squeeze into any gap, and they swayed from side to side, making one dizzy just looking at them. Besides, it was better not to make a mistake, if a mistake was made, it was always the rickshaw puller's fault, the police's calculation was that rickshaw pullers were always easier to deal with than cyclists, so they would first blame the rickshaw puller. Several times, Xiangzi really wanted to suddenly brake his cart and make the guy behind him fall. But he didn't dare, rickshaw pullers had to endure everywhere. Whenever he wanted to stomp his shoes, he had to shout: "Stop!" When they reached the front gate of Nanhai, the street was so wide, yet that bicycle was still following closely behind. Xiangzi became even more annoyed, he deliberately stopped the cart and brushed the snow off his body. He stood still, and the bicycle brushed past the cart. The person on the bicycle even looked back. Xiangzi deliberately procrastinated, waiting until the bicycle had gone far away before picking up the cart handles and cursing: "Annoying!" Mr. Cao's "humanitarianism" made him unwilling to install the wind-proof cotton canopy, and even the canvas canopy was not allowed to be put up unless there was heavy rain, in order to save the rickshaw puller some effort. With this light snow, he thought there was no need to put up the canopy, and besides, he was also eager to enjoy the night snow scene. He also noticed this bicycle, and after Xiangzi finished cursing, he said in a low voice, "If he keeps following us, don't stop at the door of my house, go to Mr. Zuo's place at Huanghua Gate; don't panic!"

Xiangzi was a little flustered. He only knew that cyclists were annoying, but he didn't realize there was something terrifying about it—since even Mr. Cao didn't dare to go home, this guy must be someone important! He ran for dozens of steps, then caught up with that person; he was deliberately waiting for him and Mr. Cao. The bicycle let Xiangzi pass, and Xiangzi glanced at the person on the bicycle. He understood at a glance, it was someone from the detective squad. He often encountered people from the squad in the teahouse, and although he had never spoken to them, he knew their demeanor and attire. This person's attire looked familiar to him: a dark blue padded coat, a woolen hat, with the hat pulled low.

At the intersection of Nanchang Street, Xiangzi took the opportunity of turning a corner to glance back, and that person was still following. He almost forgot about the snow on the ground, and put more effort into his steps. The straight, long, and bright road, with only some cold streetlights, and a detective chasing him from behind! Xiangzi had never had such an experience, he broke out in a sweat. When he reached the back gate of the park, he looked back, and he was still following! When he reached his doorstep, he didn't dare to stop, and was also a little reluctant to leave; Mr. Cao didn't say a word, so he had to continue running north. He ran all the way to the north end, and the bicycle was still following! He entered a small alley, still following! He came out of the alley, still following! Going to Huanghua Gate, he shouldn't have entered the small alley, and it wasn't until he reached the north end of the alley that he realized he had been a bit confused, and he became even more annoyed. When he ran behind Jingshan, the bicycle went north towards the back gate. Xiangzi wiped his sweat. The snow had lessened, but there were a few snowflakes mixed in with the snow pellets again. Xiangzi seemed to like snowflakes, dancing gracefully in the air, unlike the snow pellets that made people feel annoyed. He turned around and asked, "Where to, sir?"

“Still to Mr. Zuo’s residence. If anyone asks about me, tell them you don’t know me!”

“Alright!” Xiangzi’s heart pounded, but he didn’t feel it was appropriate to ask for details.

Upon arriving at Mr. Zuo’s house, Mr. Cao told Xiangzi to pull the cart inside and quickly close the door. Mr. Cao was still very calm, but his expression was not very good. After giving instructions to Xiangzi, he went inside. As soon as Xiangzi had pulled the cart into the entrance and parked it, Mr. Cao came out again, with Mr. Zuo; Xiangzi recognized him and knew that Mr. Zuo was a good friend of the household. “Xiangzi,” Mr. Cao spoke very quickly, “you take a car back. Tell my wife I’m here. Tell them to come too, by car, call another one,"No need to teach you. The car you're going to take is waiting. Understand? Good! Tell Mrs. Cao to bring the necessary things and those few paintings from the study. Did you hear me clearly? I'll call Mrs. Cao now, just to tell you again, in case she gets flustered and forgets what I said, you can remind her."

"Should I go?" Mr. Zuo asked.

"No need! That person just now might not necessarily be a detective, but I have a feeling, so I must be careful. Could you please call a car first?" Mr. Zuo went to call a car. Mr. Cao then instructed Xiangzi again, "When the car arrives, I'll pay for it. Tell Mrs. Cao to pack quickly; nothing else matters, just be sure to bring the children's things and those few paintings from the study, those few paintings! After Mrs. Cao finishes packing, tell Gao Ma to call for a car to come here. Is all this clear? After they leave, lock the main gate, move into the study to sleep, there's a phone there. Do you know how to make calls?"

"I can't make outgoing calls, but I can answer." Actually, Xiangzi didn't particularly like answering the phone either, but he didn't want to make Mr. Cao anxious, so he just agreed.

"That's fine!" Mr. Cao continued, speaking very quickly, "If there's any movement, don't open the door! We'll all be gone, leaving you alone, and they definitely won't let you go! If things look bad, turn off the lights and jump over the back wall into the Wang family's yard. Do you know the Wang family? Right! Hide at the Wang family's for a while before leaving. Don't worry about my things or your own things, just jump the wall and go, to avoid being caught! If you lose anything, I'll compensate you later. Here's five yuan for you to hold onto. Okay, I'll call Mrs. Cao, and you can tell her again later. No need to mention being caught, that cyclist just now might be a detective, or might not be; don't panic yet!"

Xiangzi's mind was in a whirl, as if he had many questions to ask, but because he was anxious to remember Mr. Cao's instructions, he dared not ask again.

The car arrived, and Xiangzi, dazed, got in. Snow was falling moderately, and things outside the car weren't very clear. He sat with his back straight, his head almost touching the roof of the car. He wanted to think things over, but his eyes were fixed on the red arrow in front of the car, so vividly and charmingly red. The small brush in front of the driver automatically swayed left and right, wiping away the mist on the glass, which was quite interesting. Just as he seemed to be tired of watching this, the car had already arrived at the house, and he got out feeling strangely uneasy.

Just as he was about to press the doorbell of the street gate, someone seemed to emerge from the wall and grabbed his wrist. Xiangzi instinctively tried to pull his hand away, but he had already recognized the person, and he stopped moving. It was the detective who had been riding the bicycle earlier.

"Xiangzi, don't you recognize me?" The detective smiled and released his hand.

Xiangzi swallowed, not knowing what to say.

"Don't you remember when you taught us to pull you to Xishan? I was that Platoon Leader Sun. Remember now?"

"Ah, Platoon Leader Sun!" Xiangzi couldn't remember. When he was dragged to the mountain by the soldiers, he didn't have time to see who was a platoon leader or a company commander.

"You don't remember me, but I remember you; that scar on your face is a good mark. I followed you for a long time just now, and at first I was a bit unsure, but looking left and right, this scar couldn't be wrong!"

"Is something wrong?" Xiangzi was about to press the doorbell again.

"Of course something's wrong, and it's important! Shall we go inside and talk?" Platoon Leader Sun—now a detective—reached out and pressed the bell.

"I'm busy!" Sweat suddenly broke out on Xiangzi's forehead, and he thought fiercely, "I can't even avoid him, how can I invite him in!"

"Don't worry, I'm here for your own good!" The detective showed a hint of a cunning smile. When Gao Ma opened the door, he stepped inside, "Excuse me, excuse me!" Without waiting for Xiangzi and Gao Ma to say a word, he pulled Xiangzi inside, pointing to the doorman's room, "You live here?" Inside the room, he glanced around, "The small room is quite clean! Your situation isn't bad!"

"Is something wrong? I'm busy!" Xiangzi couldn't listen to this idle chatter anymore. "Didn't I tell you, it's important!" Detective Sun was still smiling, but his tone was very stern. "To put it bluntly, Mr. Cao is a rebel, and he'll be shot if caught, he can't escape! We've met once, you served me in the barracks; besides, we're both people from the streets, so I'm taking a big risk to bring you a message! If you run a step late, you'll come back to a raid, and no one will escape. We work hard for a living, why get involved in their political troubles? Is that right or wrong?"

"I'd be letting Mr. Cao down!" Xiangzi was still thinking of Mr. Cao's instructions. "Letting down whom?" Detective Sun had a smile on his lips, but his eyes were sharp. "They brought the trouble upon themselves, who are you letting down? They dare to do it, they dare to face it, but it's not right for us to suffer with them! Not to mention anything else, if they lock you up for three months, you're used to being like a wild bird, and they force you into a dark room, can you stand it or not? Besides, when they go to jail, they have money to bribe their way out and won't suffer; but you, my good brother, without hard cash, you'll be tied to a piss pot! This is still a small matter, if they happen to spend money and pull some strings, they might get a few years in prison; if the officials can't explain it, they'll definitely make you the scapegoat. We don't provoke anyone, and in the end, we'll end up on the gallows, is that fair or not? You're a smart person, and smart people don't suffer immediate losses. You're letting people down, again! Let me tell you, my good brother, there's nothing in this world that's fair to us poor folks!"

Xiangzi was scared. Remembering the suffering of being dragged away by the soldiers, he could imagine the taste of being in prison. "So I have to leave, regardless of them?"

"If you care about them, who will care about you?!"

Xiangzi had no answer. After a moment of stunned silence, even his conscience nodded, "Okay, I'll go!"

"Just leave like that?" Detective Sun sneered.

Xiangzi was confused again.

"Xiangzi, my good fellow! You're too foolish! Do you think I, as a detective, would just let you go?"

"Then—" Xiangzi was so anxious he didn't know what to say.

"Don't play dumb!" Detective Sun's eyes fixed on Xiangzi, "You probably have some savings, take them out and buy your life! I don't earn as much as you in a month, I have to eat, wear clothes, and support my family, I rely on a little extra income, I'm telling you this from the heart! Think about it, can I just let you go with a wave of my hand? Friendship is friendship, if there was no friendship, would I come to persuade you? But business is business, if I don't get something out of it, do you expect my family to starve? We don't need to waste words, tell me the truth!"

"How much?" Xiangzi sat on the bed.

"As much as you have, there's no fixed price!"

"I'd rather wait to go to jail!"

"Is that what you're saying? Don't regret it?" Detective Sun's hand reached into his padded robe, "Look at this, Xiangzi! I can arrest you right now, if you resist arrest, I'll shoot! I'll take you away immediately, don't talk about money, even the clothes on your back will be stripped off as soon as you enter the prison gate. You're a smart person, think it over yourself!" "You have time to squeeze me, why don't you squeeze Mr. Cao?" Xiangzi stammered for a long time before speaking.

"He's the main culprit, catching him gets a reward, failing to catch him means trouble. You, my foolish brother, letting you go is like letting out a fart; killing you is like squashing a bug! If you pay, you go your way; if not, well, see you at Tianqiao! Don't make it difficult, be straightforward, you're a grown man! Besides, I can't keep all this money to myself, my colleagues all need a little extra, who knows how many shares there'll be. If you don't want to buy your life so cheaply, then I'm out of options! How much money do you have?"

Xiangzi stood up, his mind racing, and clenched his fists.

"You won't win if you fight, I'm telling you first, there's a whole bunch of people outside! Hurry up, give me the money! I'm giving you face, don't be ungrateful!" Detective Sun's expression became very ugly.

"Who did I provoke?!" Xiangzi said with a sob, then sat back on the edge of the bed. "You didn't provoke anyone; you just happened to be in the wrong place at the wrong time! People are born rich, we're all from the bottom. Don't say anything more!" Detective Sun shook his head, as if with infinite emotion. "Alright, consider it me wronging you, don't bother me anymore!"

Xiangzi thought for a while longer, but there was nothing he could do. His hands trembling, he pulled the sealed jar out from under the quilt.

"Let me see!" Detective Sun smiled, snatched the earthenware pot, and knocked it against the wall.

Xiangzi watched the money scatter on the ground, his heart breaking.

"Is that all?"

Xiangzi didn't make a sound, only trembled.

"Forget it! I won't go to extremes, friends are friends. But you should also know, this money buys a life, it's a cheap deal!"

Xiangzi still didn't make a sound, trembling as he tried to gather the bedding.

"Don't touch that either!"

"It's so cold..." Xiangzi's eyes blazed with anger.

"I told you not to move, so don't move! Get out!"

Xiangzi swallowed, bit his lip, pushed the door open, and walked out.

The snow was already more than an inch thick, and Xiangzi walked with his head down. Everywhere was white, only large black footprints were left behind him.

Twelve

Xiangzi wanted to find a place to sit down and think everything through, even if he could only cry after thinking, at least he would know what he was crying about; things had changed too quickly, and his mind couldn't keep up. There was no place for him to sit, everywhere was snow. The small teahouses had already closed, it was past ten o'clock; even if they were open, he wouldn't go in, he wanted to find a quiet place, he knew the tears welling up in his eyes could fall at any moment. Since there was no place to sit, he might as well walk slowly; but where to go? In this silver-white world, there was no place for him to sit, and no place for him to go; a vast expanse of white, only hungry little birds and desperate people knew what it meant to lament.

Where to go? This became a problem, no need to think about anything else for now! A small inn? No! With his clothes, he might lose something in the middle of the night, not to mention how terrible the bedbugs in the inn were. A bigger inn? He couldn't afford it, he only had five yuan, and that was his entire fortune. A bathhouse? They closed at midnight, he couldn't stay overnight. Nowhere to go.

Because he had nowhere to go, he felt his predicament even more acutely. He had been drifting in the city for several years, and all he had left was a set of clothes and five yuan; even his bedding was gone! From this, he thought about tomorrow, what would he do tomorrow? Pull a rickshaw, still pull a rickshaw, hmph, the result of pulling a rickshaw was just not finding a place to live, just having some money stolen by others! Start a small business, he only had five yuan as capital, and he would even have to buy a carrying pole and baskets, and besides, which business was guaranteed to earn enough to eat? Pulling a rickshaw could earn him thirty or forty cents on flat ground, but starting a small business required capital, and there was no guarantee of earning three meals. If he ate up all his capital and then went back to pulling a rickshaw, wouldn't that be like taking off his pants to fart, losing five yuan for nothing? This five yuan could not be easily let go of, not a single cent, this was his last hope! Become a servant, he wasn't good at it: serving people, he couldn't; washing clothes and cooking, he couldn't! He was good at nothing, knew nothing, he was just a big, clumsy, useless oaf!

Unconsciously, he arrived at Zhonghai. On the bridge, it was wide open on both sides, and as far as the eye could see, it was all snowflakes. Only then did he seem to realize that the snow hadn't stopped, he touched his head, and his woolen hat was already very wet. There was no one on the bridge, not even the police officer, who had disappeared somewhere, and a few electric lights seemed to blink constantly as the snowflakes hit them. Xiangzi looked at the snow all around, feeling lost.

He stood on the bridge for a long time, the world seemed to have died, without a sound, without a movement, the gray-white snowflakes seemed to have found an opportunity, falling frantically, lightly, continuously, wanting to bury the world without anyone knowing. In this silence, Xiangzi heard the whisper of his conscience. Don't worry about himself first, he still had to go back and check on the Cao family. Only Mrs. Cao and Gao Ma were left, not a single man! Wasn't that last five yuan given by Mr. Cao? He dared not think any further, he picked up his feet and walked back, very quickly.

There were some footprints outside the door, and two new car tracks on the road. Had Mrs. Cao already left? Why didn't that Sun fellow catch them?

He dared not go and push the door, afraid of being caught again. He looked left and right, no one, his heart pounded, let's try it, anyway he had no home to go to, so if he was caught, he was caught. He gently pushed the door, and it was open. He walked two steps along the wall, and saw the light in his own room, his own room! He wanted to cry out. He walked over, bent at the waist, listened outside the window, there was a cough inside, Gao Ma's voice! He pulled open the door. "Who? Oh, you! You scared me to death!" Gao Ma clutched her chest, composed herself, and sat on the bed. "Xiangzi, what happened?"

Xiangzi couldn't answer, he just felt as if he hadn't seen her for many years, a lump of hot air in his chest.

"What's wrong?" Gao Ma asked, looking like she was about to cry, "You hadn't come back yet, and Mr. Cao called, telling us to go to the Zuo residence, and also said you would be here soon. You came, didn't I open the door for you? I saw you with a stranger, and I didn't say a word, I quickly went in to help Mrs. Cao pack. You never came in. In the dark, Mrs. Cao and I were fumbling around, the young master was already sleeping soundly, and we had to pull him out of his warm bed. We packed the bags,"""You went to the study again to take the paintings, and you still haven't shown your face, what's wrong with you? I'm asking you! I tidied up roughly, and came out to see you, but you were gone! Madam was so angry—half of it was from anxiety—that she was trembling. I had no choice but to call for a car. But we can't just play 'empty fort strategy' and all leave. Fine, Madam and I had a big argument [1], I told Madam to go, I'll stay. If Xiangzi comes back, I'll immediately go to the Zuo residence; if he doesn't, I'll accept my fate! How could you say that! What's wrong with you, speak up!"

Xiangzi had nothing to say.

"Speak up! Does standing there dumbfounded solve anything? What exactly is going on?"

"You go!" Xiangzi finally found a sentence: "Go!" "You'll watch the house?" Gao Ma's anger subsided a little.

"When you see Mr. Cao, just say that the detective caught me, but then, but then, he didn't catch me!"
