"What kind of talk is that?" Gao Ma was so angry she almost laughed.

"Listen!" Xiangzi got angry instead: "Tell Mr. Cao to run quickly, the detective said he's sure to catch him. The Zuo residence isn't safe either. Run quickly! Once you're gone, I'll jump over to the Wang family's house and sleep there for a night. I'll lock this big gate. Tomorrow, I'll go find my own business. Sorry, Mr. Cao!"

"The more you talk, the more confused I get!" Gao Ma sighed. "Alright, I'll go, the young master might be freezing, I need to go check on him quickly! When I see Mr. Cao, I'll tell him that Xiangzi said to run quickly. Tonight Xiangzi will lock the big gate and jump over to the Wang family's to sleep; tomorrow he'll go find work. Is that right?"

Xiangzi nodded, full of shame.

After Gao Ma left, Xiangzi locked the big gate and returned to the room. The broken puzzle jar was still lying on the ground, he picked up a piece of tile and looked at it, then threw it back on the ground as before. The bedding on the bed hadn't been moved. Strange, what exactly was going on? Could it be that Detective Sun wasn't a real detective? Impossible! If Mr. Cao hadn't sensed some danger, why would he abandon his home and flee? He didn't understand! He didn't understand! He unconsciously sat down on the edge of the bed. As soon as he sat down, he stood up again as if startled. He couldn't stay here long! What if that Mr. Sun came back again?! His mind raced quickly: he felt bad for Mr. Cao, but Gao Ma bringing back the message to tell him to run quickly was still acceptable. In good conscience, Xiangzi hadn't intended to deceive anyone, and he himself was suffering. His own money was lost first, so he couldn't take care of Mr. Cao anymore. Talking to himself, he muttered while packing up his bedding.

Carrying his bedding, he put out the light and headed for the backyard. He put down the bedding, grabbed the top of the wall, and called out in a low voice, "Old Cheng! Old Cheng!" Old Cheng was the Wang family's rickshaw puller. No one answered, so Xiangzi made up his mind to jump over first. He threw the bedding over, and it landed on the snow without a sound. His heart pounded for a while. Immediately after, he climbed over the wall and jumped down. He picked up the bedding from the snow and quietly went to find Old Cheng. He knew where Old Cheng lived. Everyone seemed to be asleep, and there wasn't a sound in the entire courtyard. Xiangzi suddenly felt that being a thief wasn't a difficult thing at all, he became a little bolder, walking steadily, the snow was very firm, making a slight crunching sound. He found Old Cheng's room and coughed. Old Cheng seemed to have just lain down: "Who?"

"It's me, Xiangzi! Open the door!" Xiangzi said very naturally, softly, as if hearing Old Cheng's voice was like hearing the comfort of a loved one. Old Cheng turned on the light, wrapped in a worn fur coat, and opened the door: "What's wrong? Xiangzi! In the middle of the night!"

Xiangzi entered, placed his bedding on the ground, and sat on it, then fell silent again.

Old Cheng was over thirty, his face and body were lumpy and hard-edged. Normally, Xiangzi didn't have much of a relationship with him, but they always nodded and spoke when they met. Sometimes, when Mrs. Wang and Mrs. Cao went out shopping together, they had more opportunities to drink tea and rest together. Xiangzi greatly admired Old Cheng; Old Cheng ran very fast, but he was always flustered and seemed unable to hold the handlebars steadily. In terms of character, although Old Cheng was quite good, because of this flaw, Xiangzi could never fully admire him.

Today, Xiangzi felt Old Cheng was completely lovable. Sitting there, he couldn't say anything, but his heart was filled with gratitude and affection. Just now, he was standing on the bridge in Zhonghai; now, he was sitting in a room with an acquaintance; the drastic change made his heart feel empty; at the same time, he felt a little feverish.

Old Cheng burrowed back into his quilt, pointed at the worn fur coat, and said, "Xiangzi, smoke a cigarette, there are some in my pocket, 'Bieye' brand." The "Villa" brand cigarettes had been renamed "Bieye" by rickshaw pullers since their inception.

Xiangzi didn't usually smoke, but this time he seemed unable to refuse, so he took a cigarette and put it to his lips, puffing on it.

"What's wrong?" Old Cheng asked, "Did you quit your job?"

"No," Xiangzi remained seated on his bedding, "There was trouble! Mr. Cao's whole family ran away, and I didn't dare to watch the house alone!"

"What kind of trouble?" Old Cheng sat up again.

"I can't explain it, but it's a big mess, even Gao Ma left!"

"The four gates wide open, no one in charge?"

"I locked the main gate!"

"Humph!" Old Cheng pondered for a long time, "Should I go tell Mr. Wang?" As he spoke, he was about to put on his clothes.

"Let's talk about it tomorrow, the situation is simply inexplicable!" Xiangzi was afraid Mr. Wang would question him.

The matter Xiangzi couldn't explain was this: Mr. Cao taught a few hours of classes at a university. There was a student named Ruan Ming in the school who had always been on good terms with Mr. Cao and often came to talk to him. Mr. Cao was a socialist, and Ruan Ming's ideas were even more radical, so the two got along very well. However, their age and status caused a slight conflict: from a teacher's perspective, Mr. Cao felt he should diligently teach, and students should conscientiously complete their assignments, not being careless with grades due to personal feelings. From Ruan Ming's perspective, in this chaotic world, an ambitious young person should engage in revolutionary activities, and academic performance could be temporarily disregarded. He interacted with Mr. Cao, firstly because they got along, and secondly, he hoped to get passing grades due to their relationship, no matter how bad his exam scores were. Ambitious people in chaotic times often have a bit of a rogue streak, and history has many such excusable examples.

At the time of the exam, Mr. Cao did not give Ruan Ming a passing grade. Ruan Ming's grades, even if Mr. Cao had given him a passing mark, would have been more than enough to warrant suspension. But he particularly hated Mr. Cao. He thought Mr. Cao didn't understand "face" at all; "face," in China, had the same value as revolution. Because he was eager to do something, Ruan Ming looked down on academics. Because he looked down on academics, he gradually became accustomed to laziness, wanting to gain everyone's admiration and affection without any effort; no matter what, his ideas were progressive! Mr. Cao not giving him a passing grade clearly showed a lack of understanding for an ambitious young man; then, why bother being so friendly in the first place! Since their relationship was good normally, but then he was put in an awkward position during the exam, he thought Mr. Cao was insidious. His grades were beyond remedy, and suspension was unavoidable, so he wanted to vent his anger on Mr. Cao. Since he himself had dropped out of school, he might as well drag a teacher down with him. This way, he could make a show and demonstrate his power. Ruan Ming was not to be trifled with! Moreover, if this incident could help him join a new organization, it would be better than having nothing to do.

He compiled what Mr. Cao had said in lectures and in their casual conversations about political and social issues, and reported him to the party headquarters—that Mr. Cao was spreading radical ideas among young people.

Mr. Cao had also heard rumors, but he found it amusing. He knew how incomplete his socialism was, and he also knew how much his traditional artistic hobbies hindered radical action. How ridiculous, to actually be labeled a revolutionary mentor! How ridiculous, so he didn't pay much attention, even though students and colleagues told him to be careful. Calmness could not—in chaotic times—guarantee safety. The winter break was a good opportunity to purge the schools, and detectives began to busy themselves with investigations and arrests. Mr. Cao had felt someone following him several times. The shadow behind him changed his playful demeanor to a serious one. He had to think: this was a good opportunity to build a reputation; a few days in prison was easier and more stable than throwing a bomb, and had the same value. Imprisonment was a qualification for becoming an important person. But he refused. He refused to play along and create a false reputation for himself. In good conscience, he hated himself for not being able to become a fighter; in good conscience, he also refused to be a fake fighter. He went to see Mr. Zuo. Mr. Zuo had an idea: "When necessary, move in with me, they won't search my place!" Mr. Zuo knew people; people were more powerful than laws. "Come and stay here for a few days, hide out. We'll just admit we're afraid of them. Then we can smooth things over, it might cost a bit of money. With enough 'face' and money in hand, you can go home and everything will be fine."

Detective Sun knew that Mr. Cao often went to the Zuo residence, and also knew that when things got tight, he would definitely go there. They didn't dare to offend Mr. Zuo, but they wanted to scare Mr. Cao. If they drove him to the Zuo residence, they would have hope of getting money, and it would save face. Battering Xiangzi was not part of the detectives' plan, but since they saw Xiangzi, and it was an easy job, why not pick up a few bucks first?

Right, Xiangzi was at the "spot," he deserved it. Everyone had a way, there were cracks everywhere, only Xiangzi couldn't escape, because he was a rickshaw puller. A rickshaw puller swallowed coarse grains and bled; he had to exert the greatest effort and receive the lowest reward; he had to stand at the lowest rung of society, waiting for all people, all laws, and all hardships to strike him.

After finishing one cigarette, Xiangzi still couldn't figure it out, he was like a chicken held by a cook, only knowing that it would be good to catch his breath, with no other ideas. He really wanted to talk to Old Cheng, but he had nothing to say, his words were not enough to express his thoughts, he had experienced all the bitterness, his mouth couldn't open, like a mute. He bought a rickshaw, the rickshaw was lost; he saved money, the money was lost; all his efforts were only for others to bully him! He didn't dare to provoke anyone, he even had to avoid stray dogs, and in the end, he was still bullied to the point of being breathless! Let's not think about the past for now, what about tomorrow? He couldn't go back to the Cao residence, where could he go? "Can I sleep here for a night?" he asked, like a stray dog finding a sheltered corner, enduring for a while; but even this little thing had to be understood, to see if it would bother others.

"You just stay here, where would you go in this freezing weather? Is the floor okay? You can squeeze in too!"

Xiangzi refused to squeeze in, the floor was perfectly fine.

Old Cheng fell asleep, but Xiangzi tossed and turned, unable to sleep. The cold from the floor quickly froze the mattress like a sheet of iron, he curled his legs, his calves seemed to be cramping. The cold wind coming through the door crack pricked his head like a swarm of tiny needles. He closed his eyes tightly, covered his head, but couldn't sleep. Listening to Old Cheng's snoring, he felt anxious, wishing he could get up and hit Old Cheng to feel better. It got colder and colder, his throat itched from the cold, and he was afraid of coughing and waking Old Cheng.

Unable to sleep, he really wanted to secretly get up and go back to the Cao residence to check. Anyway, the matter was blown, and there was no one in the courtyard, so why not go get a few things? The few coins he had saved with such difficulty were stolen, stolen because of the Cao residence, why couldn't he steal some things? He lost money because of the Cao residence, and then the Cao residence compensated him, wasn't that just right? Thinking this, his eyes lit up, and he instantly forgot the cold; go! The money that was so hard to get, lost, and then so easy to get back, go!

He had already sat up, then quickly lay back down, as if Old Cheng was watching him! His heart pounded. No, he couldn't be a thief, he couldn't! He had just cleared himself, not doing what Mr. Cao had instructed, which was already letting someone down; how could he go and steal from him? He couldn't go! He'd rather starve to death than steal!

How did he know others wouldn't steal? Who would know if that Mr. Sun took some things? He sat up again. A dog barked a few times in the distance. He lay down again. Still couldn't go, if others stole, let them steal, his own conscience was clear. He was so poor, he couldn't let his heart have another black mark!

Besides, Gao Ma knew he came to the Wang family's, if things were lost during the night, it would be him even if it wasn't him, and it would be him even if it wasn't him! Not only did he not want to steal, but he was also afraid of others going in. If things were really lost that night, he wouldn't be able to clear his name even if he jumped into the Yellow River! He wasn't cold anymore, in fact, his palms were a little sweaty. What to do? Jump back into the residence to watch? He didn't dare. His life was bought with money, he couldn't walk into a trap again. If he didn't go, what if things were lost?

He couldn't think of an idea. He sat up again, sitting with his legs bent, his head almost touching his knees. His head was very heavy, and his eyes wanted to close, but he didn't dare to sleep. The night was so long, but Xiangzi didn't have a moment to close his eyes.

He sat for an unknown amount of time, changing his mind countless times. Suddenly, an idea flashed in his mind, and he reached out to push Old Cheng: "Old Cheng! Old Cheng! Wake up!"

"What do you want?" Old Cheng was very reluctant to open his eyes: "To pee, there's a chamber pot under the bed."

"Wake up!""Turn on the light!"

"Is there a thief or something?" Old Cheng sat up groggily.

"Are you fully awake?"

"Mm!"

"Old Cheng, look! This is my bedding, these are my clothes, this is the five yuan Mr. Cao gave me; is there nothing else?"

"No, why?" Old Cheng yawned.

"Are you fully awake? These are all my things, I didn't take a single blade of grass or a single tree from the Cao family?"

"No! We brothers, who have eaten at big houses for a long time, can we still be sticky-fingered? If we can do it, we do it; if we can't, we don't; we can't take other people's things! Is that what this is about?"

"Did you see clearly?"

Old Cheng smiled, "That's right! I say, aren't you cold?"

"Alright!"

[1]

 A horizontal strike across the bridge of the nose, meaning a guarantee.

Thirteen

Because of the snow's glow, the day seemed to break a little earlier. As the year-end approached, many families bought chickens to raise, and their crowing was several times more frequent than usual. Chickens crowed everywhere, creating a scene of a prosperous year with auspicious snow. Xiangzi, however, hadn't slept well all night. In the latter half of the night, he managed a few naps, half-asleep and half-awake, bobbing up and down like he was floating on water, his heart uneasy. The colder he got, the more he heard the chickens crowing, and he simply couldn't bear it anymore. Unwilling to disturb Old Cheng, he curled his legs, used the quilt to muffle his cough, and dared not get up. He endured, he waited, his heart filled with extreme anxiety. Finally, when dawn broke and he heard the sound of large cartwheels and the shouts of carters in the street, he sat up. Sitting was still cold, so he stood up, buttoned his clothes, and opened a crack in the door to look outside. The snow wasn't very deep; it had probably stopped falling in the middle of the night. The sky seemed clear, but it was hazy and indistinct, even the snow had a faint gray shadow over it. He immediately saw the large footprints he had left last night; although they were covered by snow again, the depressions were still very clear.

Firstly, to have something to do, and secondly, to erase the traces, he didn't make a sound. He fumbled for a broom in the corner of the room and went to sweep the snow. The snow was heavy and not easy to sweep. He couldn't find a large bamboo broom for a while, so he bent very low and scraped hard. The top layer was swept away, but some snow pellets remained clinging to the ground, as if they had gripped the earth. Straightening his back twice, he swept the entire outer courtyard clean, piling all the snow under the two small willow trees. He broke a slight sweat, felt warm, and a little lighter. Stamping his feet, he let out a long, white breath.

Entering the room, he put the broom back in its place and thought about tidying up his bedding. Old Cheng woke up, yawned, and before he could close his mouth, he spoke, "Isn't it early?" His tone was very complex. After speaking, he wiped away a tear and casually pulled out a cigarette from his fur coat pocket. After two puffs, he was fully awake. "Xiangzi, don't leave yet! Let me go get some hot water, and we'll have a hot pot of tea. You must have had a tough night!"

"Shall I go?" Xiangzi also offered politely. But as soon as he said it, he remembered the terror of last night, and his heart suddenly felt a lump.

"No, I'll go! I still need to treat you!" Saying this, Old Cheng quickly put on his clothes, leaving all the buttons undone, only tying a sash around his tattered fur coat. He ran out with a cigarette in his mouth, "Ho! The courtyard is all swept? You're really something! I'll treat you!" Xiangzi felt a little better.

After a while, Old Cheng returned, carrying two large bowls of sweet gruel, and an unknown number of horseshoe pastries and small fried dough sticks. "No tea, let's have some gruel first. Come, eat! If it's not enough, I'll buy more. If there's no money, we can get it on credit. Just don't pout when doing hard work, come on!"

The day was fully bright, and the room was cold, clear, and bright. The two of them held their bowls and drank, making loud but sweet sounds. Neither spoke, finishing the pastries and fried dough sticks in one go. "How was it?" Old Cheng picked a sesame seed from his teeth.

"It's time to go!" Xiangzi looked at the bedding roll on the ground.

"Tell me, I still don't understand what happened!" Old Cheng offered Xiangzi a cigarette, but Xiangzi shook his head.

After thinking for a moment, Xiangzi felt embarrassed not to tell Old Cheng everything. Stammering, he recounted what had happened last night. Although it was difficult, his account was not incomplete.

Old Cheng pursed his lips for a long time, as if trying to savor the taste of it. "In my opinion, you should still go find Mr. Cao. This matter can't just be left like this, and the money can't just be lost! Didn't you just say that Mr. Cao instructed you to run if things looked bad? So, if you were blocked by detectives as soon as you got off the car, whose fault is that? It's not that you're disloyal, it's that things came too suddenly and wickedly, and you had no choice but to look out for your own life first! As I see it, there's nothing to feel sorry about. Go, find Mr. Cao, and tell him everything that happened from beginning to end. I think he won't blame you, and by chance, he might even compensate you for your money! Go, leave your bedding here, and go find him early. The day is short; it'll be eight o'clock as soon as the sun comes out, hurry up and go!"

Xiangzi's spirits lifted. He still felt a little bad about Mr. Cao, but Old Cheng's words were very reasonable—how could he worry about the Cao family's affairs when detectives were blocking him with guns? "Go!" Old Cheng urged again. "I think you were a bit confused last night. When urgent matters arise, no one can guarantee they won't lose their head. The path I'm giving you now is definitely good. I'm a bit older than you, so I've been through more things. Go, isn't the sun out now?"

A sliver of sunlight from the rising sun, aided by the snow, had already illuminated the entire city. Blue sky, white snow, light in the sky, light on the snow, a golden sparkle between the blue and white, making it exhilarating to the point of being unable to open one's eyes! Just as Xiangzi was about to leave, someone knocked on the door. Old Cheng went to see and called from the doorway, "Xiangzi! Someone's looking for you!"

Wang Er from the Zuo residence, his nose dripping with clear water from the cold, stomped the snow off his feet in the doorway. When Old Cheng saw Xiangzi come out, he said, "Come inside, all of you!" The three of them entered the room together.

"Well, uh," Wang Er said, rubbing his hands, "I came to check on the house, how do I get in? The main gate is locked. Well, uh, it's cold after the snow, really cold! Well, uh, Mr. Cao, Mrs. Cao, they all left early this morning. To Tianjin, maybe Shanghai, I'm not sure. Mr. Zuo asked me to check on the house. Well, uh, it's really cold!"

Xiangzi suddenly felt like crying! He was just about to follow Old Cheng's advice and go find Mr. Cao, but Mr. Cao had left. After a long moment of stunned silence, he asked, "Did Mr. Cao say anything about me?"

"Well, uh, no. They all got up before dawn, practically no time to talk. The train, uh, leaves at 7:40! Well, uh, how do I get across that courtyard?" Wang Er was eager to get across.

"Jump over!" Xiangzi glanced at Old Cheng, as if handing Wang Er over to him, and picked up his bedding roll.

"Where are you going?" Old Cheng asked.

"Renhe Factory, there's nowhere else to go!" This sentence expressed all the grievances, shame, and helplessness in Xiangzi's heart. He had no other choice but to surrender! All paths were blocked; he could only search for that black-tower-like Huniu on the white snow. He valued his reputation, was strong, loyal, and righteous; none of it was of any use, because he had a "dog's" life!

Old Cheng took it over: "You go your way. This isn't in front of Wang Er, you haven't touched a single thing in the Cao residence! Go. When you come to this street, come in and chat for a while, maybe I'll hear some good news and even recommend you. After you leave, I'll take Wang Er over there. Do you have coal?"

"Coal, firewood, they're all in the small room in the back courtyard." Xiangzi hoisted his bedding.

The snow on the street was no longer so white; on the road, it had been pressed down by cartwheels, revealing a hint of ice color. On the dirt road, it had been trampled by horses, already black and white in patches, quite a pity. Xiangzi didn't think about anything, just carried his bedding and walked forward. He walked straight to the Renhe Car Factory. He didn't dare to stop; he knew that if he stopped, he wouldn't have the courage to go in. He walked straight in, his face burning hot. He had prepared a sentence to say to Huniu: "I'm here, just deal with it! Whatever you do is fine, I have no other way!" But when he saw her, he turned this sentence over in his mind several times, but he could never say it; his mouth wasn't that quick.

Huniu had just gotten up, her hair disheveled, her eyelids a bit swollen, and a layer of small white goosebumps on her dark face, like a frozen chicken with its feathers plucked.

"Oh! You're back!" She was very affectionate, and her eyes sparkled with a smile. "Rent me a cart!" Xiangzi lowered his head, looking at the un-melted snow on the tip of his shoe.

"Go talk to the old man," she whispered, then nodded towards the east room.

Liu Siye was drinking tea in the room, with a large white stove in front of him, its flames more than half a foot high. Seeing Xiangzi enter, he said with a mix of annoyance and amusement, "You rascal, you're still alive?! You forgot about me! Count, how many days has it been since you last came? How are things? Did you buy a cart?"

Xiangzi shook his head, a stinging pain in his heart. "I still need a cart to pull, Fourth Master!"

"Hmph, the deal fell through again! Alright, go pick one yourself!" Liu Siye poured a bowl of tea, "Come, drink a bowl first."

Xiangzi picked up the bowl and stood in front of the stove, drinking in large gulps. The tea was very hot, the fire was very hot, and he felt a little sleepy. He put down the bowl and was about to leave when Liu Siye called him back.

"Wait, what's the hurry? Let me tell you: you've come at just the right time. The twenty-seventh is my birthday, and I'm going to put up a canopy and invite guests. You can help out for a few days, no need to pull a cart for now. They," Liu Siye pointed towards the courtyard, "are all unreliable, I don't want them to mess around. You help out. Do what needs to be done, don't wait for me to tell you. First, go sweep the snow, and I'll treat you to hotpot at noon." "Yes, Fourth Master!" Xiangzi had made up his mind. Since he was back here, he would leave everything to the Liu family father and daughter. They could move him around however they liked; he had accepted his fate! "Didn't I say so?" Huniu came in, holding the time [1], "It's still Xiangzi, everyone else is a bit lacking."

Liu Siye smiled. Xiangzi lowered his head even further.

"Come, Xiangzi!" Huniu called him out, "Here's some money, go buy a broom first, a bamboo one, good for sweeping snow. You need to sweep quickly, the canopy builders are coming today." In her room, as she counted money for Xiangzi, she whispered, "Be energetic! Get the old man to like you! Our matter has hope!"

Xiangzi didn't say anything, nor did he get angry. He seemed to have given up, not thinking about anything, just letting one day blend into another. If there was food, he ate; if there was drink, he drank; if there was work, he did it, keeping his hands and feet busy. A few turns and a day was gone. He was like a donkey pulling a millstone, knowing nothing when asked, only knowing how to walk with the millstone.

But he also realized that he would never be truly happy. Although he refused to think, refused to speak, refused to get angry, there was always something stuck in his heart. He would temporarily forget it while working, but as soon as he had a moment of leisure, he would feel this thing—soft, yet always so large; without any definite taste, yet choking, like a sponge. With this thing stuck in his heart, he forced himself to work, so that he would be too tired to move, and could fall into a deep sleep. He left the night's affairs to dreams, and the day's affairs to his hands and feet. He was like a dead man who could work. He swept snow, he bought things, he ordered gas lamps, he washed carts, he moved tables and chairs, he ate Liu Siye's celebratory meals, he slept, he knew nothing, no words in his mouth, no thoughts in his mind, only vaguely feeling that sponge-like thing!

The snow on the ground was swept clean, and the snow on the roof gradually melted away. The canopy builders "called out" as they went onto the roof and set up the framework. It was agreed to be a warm canopy covering the courtyard, with three sides of eaves, three sides of railings, and three sides of glass windows. Inside the canopy, there were glass partitions, hanging screens, and all visible wood was wrapped in red cloth. The main and side entrances were all decorated with colorful streamers, and the kitchen was set up in the back courtyard. Liu Siye, celebrating his ninety-ninth birthday, wanted to make it a lively affair, so the first priority was to set up a decent canopy. The day was short, so the canopy builders only finished the main structure, put up the railings and cloth. The decorative work inside the canopy and the colorful streamers on the doors would be hung the next morning. Liu Siye lost his temper greatly with the canopy builders over this, his face flushed with anger. Because of this, he sent Xiangzi to rush the gas lamps and the cook, emphasizing that there must be no delays. In fact, these two things would definitely not be delayed, but the old man was not at ease. Xiangzi had just run back for this when Liu Siye again sent him to borrow mahjong tiles, three or four sets, as they absolutely had to gamble heartily on the day. After borrowing the tiles, he was again sent to borrow a gramophone, as a birthday celebration always needed some noise. Xiangzi's legs didn't stop for a moment, running until eleven at night. Accustomed to pulling carts, walking empty-handed was even more tiring than running. On his last trip back, even he, even he, could barely lift his feet. "Good boy! You're something! If I had a son like you, it would be good even if it meant I lived a few years less! Go rest, there's still work tomorrow!"

Huniu, standing nearby, winked at Xiangzi.

The next morning, the canopy builders came to finish the work. The colorful screens were hung, depicting battle scenes from "Romance of the Three Kingdoms": the three heroes fighting Lu Bu, Changbanpo, the burning of the linked camps, and so on, with all the painted-face generals on horseback, wielding swords and spears. Old Liu looked up and down, feeling very satisfied. Immediately after, the furniture shop arrived to unload their goods: eight seats were placed in the canopy, and the aprons, chair cushions, and stool covers were all embroidered in bright red. A birthday altar was placed in the main hall, with incense burners and candle holders all made of cloisonné, and four red felt mats in front of the table. Old Liu immediately told Xiangzi to go get a set of apples. Huniu secretly slipped him two yuan and told him to order birthday peaches and longevity noodles, with a set of eight immortals on the birthday peaches, to be presented as if from Xiangzi. The apples were bought and immediately arranged. After a short while, the birthday peaches and longevity noodles also arrived, placed behind the apples. The large birthday peaches had red tips and were adorned with the eight immortals, looking very grand.

"Xiangzi sent these, see how thoughtful he is!" Huniu whispered boastfully into her father's ear, and Liu Siye smiled at Xiangzi.

A large "Longevity" character was still missing from the center of the birthday altar. Traditionally, this was a gift from friends and did not need to be prepared by oneself. Since no one had sent one yet, Liu Siye, impatient, was about to lose his temper again: "For anyone's weddings or funerals, I'm always at the forefront, but when it comes to my own affairs, I'm left high and dry,“Damn it!”

“Tomorrow is the twenty-sixth, the banquet has just begun, what’s the rush?” Huniu shouted, trying to comfort him. “I want to set everything out at once; seeing it in bits and pieces like this is agonizing! I tell you, Xiangzi, the gas lamps [2]

 must be installed today. If they’re not here by four o’clock, I’ll skin them alive!”

“Xiangzi, go and urge them again!” Huniu deliberately relied on him, always calling Xiangzi to do things in front of her father. Xiangzi didn’t say a word, understood the message, and left.

“It’s not that I’m saying it, old man,” she said, pouting slightly, “if I had a son, he’d either be like me or like Xiangzi! It’s a pity I was born into the wrong body. But there’s nothing to be done about that. Actually, having Xiangzi as a godson isn’t bad! Look at him, he doesn’t even fart all day, but he gets everything done!”

Liu Siye didn’t respond, thought for a moment, then said, “Where’s the gramophone? Let’s have some music!”

The broken gramophone, borrowed from who knows where, made every sound screech like a cat’s tail being stepped on, piercing to the bone! Liu Siye didn’t care, as long as there was some noise.

By the afternoon, everything was ready, just waiting for the chef to arrive the next day to set up. Liu Siye inspected everything, finding flowers and greenery everywhere, and nodded to himself. That evening, he invited Mr. Feng from Tianshun Coal Shop to manage the accounts. Mr. Feng, a Shanxi native, was meticulous with accounts. Mr. Feng immediately came to look around, told Xiangzi to buy two red account books and a sheet of red paper. Cutting the red paper, he wrote some longevity characters and pasted them everywhere. Liu Siye felt that Mr. Feng was truly thoughtful, and immediately wanted to invite him for a few rounds of mahjong. Mr. Feng knew Liu Siye’s formidable reputation and didn’t dare accept. The mahjong game didn’t happen, and Liu Siye felt a bit annoyed. He called over a few rickshaw pullers, “Play ‘bao’ [a gambling game], do you have the guts?”

Everyone was willing to play, but they didn’t have the guts to play with Liu Siye; who didn’t know he used to run a gambling den!

“You bunch of good-for-nothings, how did you even survive!” Siye lost his temper. “When I was your age, I dared to do anything even without a penny in my pocket, and worried about losing later; come on!”

“With coppers?” a rickshaw puller tentatively asked.

“Keep your coppers, Liu Si doesn’t play games with kids!” The old man gulped down a cup of tea and rubbed his bald head. “Forget it, I won’t invite you anymore! I tell you, go tell everyone: tomorrow the banquet begins, relatives and friends will arrive by late afternoon, all rickshaws must be parked by four o’clock, no pushing and shoving with rickshaws coming in and out! Tomorrow’s rickshaw rental fee is waived, park by four. I’m letting you pull rickshaws for free for a day, so wish me well in your hearts, don’t be heartless! The day after tomorrow is the main day, no one is allowed to pull rickshaws. At 8:30 AM, I’ll set out food for you first, six large bowls, two seven-inch dishes, four small side dishes, one hotpot; you’ll be well-fed! All of you wear long gowns, anyone who comes in short and scruffy will be kicked out! After eating, all of you get out, so I can entertain my relatives and friends. Relatives and friends will eat three large bowls, six cold appetizers, six stir-fries, four large bowls, one hotpot. I’m explaining this clearly beforehand, don’t look on enviously. Relatives and friends are relatives and friends; I don’t want anything from you. Those with a heart can give me ten silver dollars as a gift, I won’t mind if it’s little; if you don’t bring a single coin, just kowtow to me three times, I’ll accept it too. Just be well-behaved, understand? If you want to eat with me again in the evening, come back after six o’clock, whatever is left is yours; coming back early is not allowed! Understand?” “Tomorrow there are late-night fares, Siye,” a middle-aged rickshaw puller asked, “why do we have to park by four o’clock?”

“Those with late-night fares can come back after eleven! Just don’t push and shove when there are people in the shed! You pull rickshaws, Liu Si is not in the same business as you, understand?”

Everyone had nothing more to say, but couldn’t find a way to leave, standing there felt awkward; Liu Siye’s words made everyone feel a knot of anger and injustice in their hearts. Although a day’s waived rickshaw fee was a benefit, who would eat for free? At the very least, they’d have to give a gift of forty coppers; moreover, Liu Si’s words were so unpleasant, as if for his birthday celebration, they all had to hide like mice. Furthermore, on the main day, the twenty-seventh, everyone was forbidden to go out with their rickshaws, right when business was good at the end of the year. Liu Si could afford to sacrifice a day’s income, but everyone else couldn’t endure “wasting” [3]

 a day! Everyone stood there, angry but not daring to speak, not wishing Liu Siye well in their hearts.

Huniu tugged Xiangzi, and Xiangzi followed her out.

Everyone’s anger seemed to suddenly find an outlet, all staring at Xiangzi’s retreating figure. For the past two days, everyone felt that Xiangzi was Liu’s lackey, desperately currying favor, working tirelessly as a handyman [4]

. Xiangzi didn’t know any of this; he helped the Liu family to distract himself from his worries; he had nothing to say to everyone in the evening, because there was nothing to say. They didn’t know his grievances, and thought he had ingratiated himself with Liu Siye, so he disdained talking to them. Huniu’s attention to Xiangzi particularly soured everyone’s mood; thinking of the current situation, Liu Siye forbade them from moving around in the celebration tent, but Xiangzi would surely get to eat well all day; they were all rickshaw pullers, so why were there different classes? Look, Miss Liu called Xiangzi out again! Everyone’s eyes followed Xiangzi, and their legs also wanted to move, so they all made excuses to walk out. Miss Liu was talking to Xiangzi under the gas lamp, and everyone nodded to each other.

[1]

 “拿着时候” (ná zhe shí hou), meaning estimating that a suitable time has arrived.

[2]

 “水月灯” (shuǐ yuè dēng), meaning gas lamp.

[3]

 “泡” (pào), meaning to waste time. It is a deliberate action.

[4]

 “碎催” (suì cuī), meaning a handyman or odd-job man.

Fourteen

The Liu family’s event was very lively. Liu Siye was very satisfied that so many people came to kowtow and wish him a happy birthday. What made him even prouder was that many old friends also came to congratulate him. From these old friends, he saw that his event was not only lively but also “improved.” The clothes of those old friends were outdated, while Siye’s fur robe and mandarin jacket were all new. Professionally speaking, several friends were wealthier than him back then, but now—after the changes of the past two or three decades—they had fallen lower and lower, some even struggling to get enough to eat. Looking at them, and then at his own celebration tent, the longevity hall, the hanging screen depicting Changbanpo, and the three large bowls of food, he felt that he was indeed a cut above them, he had “improved.” Even for gambling, he had prepared mahjong tiles, which seemed much more refined than playing “bao.” However, in this lively scene, he also felt a touch of desolation and sadness. Accustomed to a solitary life, he had originally thought that the people who would come on his birthday would only be shopkeepers and gentlemen from businesses, and the rough-and-tumble acquaintances he had made over the years. He hadn’t expected some female guests to also come. Although Huniu could entertain them for him, he suddenly felt his own loneliness, without a wife, only a daughter, and she looked like a man. If Huniu had been a man, of course, she would have long since married and had children, and even if he were an old widower, he might not be so lonely. Yes, he lacked nothing but a son. The older he got, the smaller his hope of having a son became. A birthday celebration was supposed to be a joyous occasion, but it also seemed like a time for tears. No matter how much he had improved, if no one continued his legacy, wouldn’t everything still be in vain?

In the morning, he was extremely pleased. Everyone wished him a happy birthday, and he accepted it grandly, as if he felt himself to be an old hero who had emerged victorious. In the afternoon, his spirits sagged a bit. Looking at the children brought by the female guests, he was both envious and jealous, and yet dared not get close to them. Not getting close made him feel awkward. He wanted to throw a tantrum, but he wouldn’t let it erupt immediately; he knew he was a man of the world and couldn’t make a fool of himself in front of relatives and friends. He wished this day would pass quickly so he wouldn’t have to suffer anymore.

There was still a slight imperfection: in the morning, when food was served to the rickshaw pullers, Xiangzi almost got into a fight.

Dinner started after eight, and the rickshaw pullers were a bit unwilling. Although yesterday’s rickshaw rental fee was waived for a day, no one came to eat empty-handed today; whether it was a dime or forty coppers, everyone brought a gift. On weekdays, they were poor laborers, and Liu Si was the boss; today, in their eyes, they were guests and should not be treated this way.

Moreover, they had to leave right after eating, and weren’t allowed to take out their rickshaws, right at the end of the year!

Xiangzi knew he wasn’t among those who had to leave right after eating, but he wanted to eat with everyone. Firstly, he wanted to finish eating early to get things done, and secondly, to appear friendly. As he sat down with everyone, they all shifted their dissatisfaction with Liu Si onto him. As soon as he sat down, someone said, “Hey, you’re an honored guest, why are you sitting with us?” Xiangzi gave a silly smile, not understanding the implication of the words. For the past few days, he hadn’t spoken any idle words himself, so his mind also seemed to be less active. Everyone dared not vent their anger at Liu Si, so they could only eat a bit more of his food; vegetables couldn’t be refilled, but the wine had no limit, celebratory wine! They tacitly decided to use wine to vent their anger. Some drank silently, others played finger-guessing games; old man Liu couldn’t stop them from playing. Xiangzi watched everyone drink, and feeling it would be too out of place not to join, he also drank two cups. As they drank, everyone’s eyes turned red, and their mouths were no longer restrained. Some said, “Xiangzi, Camel, your job is great! Eating all day, serving the master and young lady! Tomorrow you won’t have to pull a rickshaw, it’s best to become a personal attendant!” Xiangzi understood a bit of the meaning, but he didn’t take it to heart; from the moment he entered Renhe Factory, he had decided not to be a hero anymore, leaving everything to fate. Let whoever wanted to say whatever they wanted. He suppressed his anger. Others said, “Xiangzi is taking a different path, we earn money by brute force, Xiangzi relies on internal skill!” Everyone burst into laughter. Xiangzi felt that everyone was “biting” at him, but he had endured such great grievances, why bother with a few idle words? He still didn’t make a sound. People at the next table saw an opportunity, some craned their necks and shouted, “Xiangzi, when you become the boss tomorrow, don’t forget your brothers!” Xiangzi still didn’t speak, and people at his own table said again, “Speak up, Camel!”

Xiangzi’s face turned red, and he said in a low voice, “How can I become the boss?!” “Hmph, why can’t you? It’s going to be ‘dong dong cha’ [1]

 soon!” Xiangzi didn’t understand what “dong dong cha” meant, but he instinctively guessed it referred to his relationship with Huniu. His face slowly turned from red to white, and all the grievances he had suffered before suddenly came to mind, all blocked in his heart. The tolerance and silence of several days seemed unsustainable, like water that had been held back, ready to burst out at an outlet. Just at this moment, a rickshaw puller pointed at his face and said, “Xiangzi, I’m talking about you, you’re truly ‘a mute eating dumplings—you know what’s what in your heart.’ Isn’t that right, tell us yourself, Xiangzi? Xiangzi?”

Xiangzi suddenly stood up, his face pale, and asked the man, “Let’s go outside and talk, do you dare?”

Everyone was stunned. They indeed intended to “bite” at him, to make some sarcastic remarks, but they hadn’t prepared for a fight.

Suddenly there was silence, like forest birds chirping suddenly seeing an eagle. Xiangzi stood alone, much taller than the others, feeling his isolation. But with anger in his heart, he seemed to deeply believe that even if all of them attacked, they would not be his match. He added, “Does anyone dare to come out?”

Everyone suddenly understood, almost in unison: “Alright, Xiangzi, we were just teasing you!”

Liu Siye saw it: “Sit down, Xiangzi!” Then to everyone, “Don’t bully anyone just because they’re honest. If I get angry, I’ll kick all of you out! Eat quickly!” Xiangzi left his seat. Everyone glanced at old man Liu out of the corner of their eyes, and all picked up their food. Before long, they started chattering again, like forest birds whose danger had passed, chirping softly again.

Xiangzi squatted at the doorway for a long time, waiting for them. If any of them dared to say idle words again, he would beat them! He had nothing left, so he might as well not care about the consequences [2]

! But everyone came out in groups of three or five, and didn’t look for him again. Although no fight happened, he did vent some anger. Then he thought, this action today had offended many people. On weekdays, he had no close friends to begin with, which was why he had nowhere to express his grievances; how could he offend more people? He felt a bit regretful. The food he had just eaten was heavy in his stomach, causing a slight pain. He stood up, thinking, what the hell, don’t those people who fight and cause trouble every other day also live quite interestingly? Is being honest and well-behaved necessarily good? With this thought, he drew a different path for himself in his heart. Xiangzi on this path was completely different from what he had hoped for before. This was a Xiangzi who made friends with everyone he met, took advantage everywhere, drank other people’s tea, smoked other people’s cigarettes, borrowed money and didn’t return it, didn’t dodge cars, urinated wherever he pleased, played tricks on policemen all day long, and didn’t mind staying in the “district” for two or three days. Yes, such rickshaw pullers also lived, and were happy, at least happier than Xiangzi. Alright, if being honest, well-behaved, and strong-willed were all useless, then becoming such a scoundrel wasn’t bad either. Not only not bad, Xiangzi thought, but it even had a certain heroic spirit, fearing nothing, absolutely not bowing his head and suffering in silence. That’s right! That’s how it should be done! Bad apples are made from good people. He even regretted a little that this fight hadn’t happened. Fortunately, there was no rush, from now on, he would never bow his head to anyone again.Liu Siye didn't tolerate any nonsense. Putting together everything he had heard and seen, he understood most of it. These past few days, the girl had been exceptionally obedient, hmph, because Xiangzi was back! He saw her eyes constantly following him. The old man kept this matter to himself, feeling even more desolate and sad. Just think, he had no son to begin with, so he couldn't build a vibrant family; and now his daughter was leaving with someone! His life's efforts would be wasted! Xiangzi was indeed a good man, but when it came to being a son-in-law, he was far from it; a stinking rickshaw puller! He had toiled his whole life, fought in brawls, knelt on iron chains, and now, at the end, he was supposed to let some country bumpkin take his daughter and all his property? No such easy thing! Even if there were, it wouldn't come from Liu Si! Liu Si had been a tough nut since childhood!

Around three or four in the afternoon, some people came to offer birthday wishes, but the old man already felt bored. The more guests praised his vigor and good fortune, the more meaningless he found it.

After dark, guests gradually dispersed, with only a dozen or so close friends and those living nearby remaining. Looking at the empty canopy in the courtyard, illuminated by the moonlight and appearing bluish, and the tables with their aprons removed, the old man felt empty and bored, as if he were seeing his own death, which would be no different, just a festive canopy replaced by a white one, with no children or grandchildren mourning before his coffin, only some irrelevant people playing mahjong and keeping vigil! He really wanted to kick out the remaining guests; while he still had a breath in him, he should assert his authority! But, after all, it wasn't right to vent his anger on friends. His anger then turned, and the more he looked at his daughter, the more displeased he became. Xiangzi was sitting in the canopy, looking quite presentable, his scar illuminated by the lamp like a piece of jade. The old man found this pair increasingly irritating!

Huniu had always been wild and unrestrained, but today she was dressed up from head to toe, and had to feign politeness with guests, both to earn everyone's praise and to show off in front of Xiangzi. In the morning, she found it somewhat interesting, but by noon, feeling a bit tired, she grew annoyed and even wanted to find someone to scold. By evening, she had no patience left, her eyebrows constantly furrowed.

It was past seven o'clock, and Liu Siye felt a bit sleepy, but he refused to admit his age and wouldn't go to bed. Everyone invited him to play a few rounds of cards, but he wouldn't say he was too tired, instead claiming that cards weren't fun, and that he preferred gambling or Pai Gow. Since no one wanted to change games midway, he just sat by himself. To perk himself up, he drank a few more cups, repeatedly saying he hadn't eaten enough, and complaining that the cook charged too much and the dishes weren't substantial. From this point on, he completely overturned all the satisfaction he had felt during the day: the canopy, the furniture, the cook, and everything else weren't worth that much money, they had all ripped him off, it was all a scam!

Mr. Feng, the accountant, had already settled the accounts: twenty-five congratulatory scrolls, three sets of birthday peaches and noodles, a jar of birthday wine, two pairs of birthday candles, and about twenty yuan in gift money. The number of items was not small, but most were for forty copper coins or one dime.

Hearing this report, Liu Siye became even angrier. If he had known this, he should have prepared "fried noodles"! Eating a feast from three large bowls, and only giving a dime as a gift? This was simply treating the old man like a fool! From now on, he would never host another event, he couldn't afford to lose such humiliating money! Needless to say, everyone, relatives and friends alike, wanted to eat for free; a sixty-nine-year-old man, smart his whole life, but foolish for a moment, letting a bunch of monkeys and bastards take advantage of him! The old man grew angrier the more he thought about it, even counting his daytime satisfaction as his own foolishness; thinking this, he muttered aloud, uttering many curses no longer common on the streets.

Friends hadn't all left yet, and Huniu, to save face for everyone, wanted to stop her father's ranting. But seeing everyone focused on their cards, seemingly not paying attention to what the old man was muttering, she found it inconvenient to speak, lest she make things clearer. Let him ramble, everyone would just let it pass.

Who knew, the old man's rambling eventually turned to her. She decided she wouldn't tolerate it! He was celebrating his birthday, and she had been busy for days, only to end up with nothing good, she couldn't give in! Sixty-nine or seventy-nine, it didn't matter, he had to be reasonable! She immediately retorted:

"You want to spend money on this event yourself, what does it have to do with me?"

The old man met with a counterattack, his spirit suddenly invigorated. "What does it have to do with you? It's all about you! Do you think my eyes don't mind their own business?"

"What did you see? I've been tired all day, and now you're taking it out on me, wait a minute! Tell me, what did you see?" Huniu's fatigue also dissipated, and her tongue became very sharp.

"Don't look at me doing things, you're jealous! See? I've seen it all along, hmph!"

"Why am I jealous?!" she said, shaking her head. "What exactly did you see?"

"Isn't that it?!" Liu Si pointed into the canopy—Xiangzi was bending over sweeping the floor. "Him?" Huniu's heart trembled, she hadn't expected the old man's eyes to be so sharp. "Hmph! What about him?"

"Don't pretend to be ignorant when you know perfectly well!" The old man stood up. "Either him or me, either me or him, I'll tell you straight. I'm your father! I have the right to interfere!"

Huniu hadn't expected things to break so quickly, her plan was less than halfway through, and the old man had already hit the nail on the head! What to do? Her face flushed, a dark red, combined with the half-worn powder and the bright lamplight, it looked like a piece of overcooked pig liver, complex and ugly in color. She felt a bit tired; this provocation, coupled with her rising anger, left her unable to think of a solution, her mind in turmoil. She couldn't just back down like this, even in turmoil, she had to find a solution immediately. Even the most inappropriate idea was better than no idea, she never yielded to anyone! Alright, let's just get it over with, good or bad, it all depended on this one strike! "It's good to clear things up today, let's just consider it this way, what do you say? I'd like to hear it! This is you asking for trouble, don't say I'm being mean to you!"

The card players seemed to hear the father and daughter arguing, but they couldn't bear to be distracted. To drown out their voices, everyone slammed their cards louder and shouted "red," "pong"... Xiangzi had understood what was happening, and continued sweeping the floor with his head down. He had a plan; if things went south, he'd fight!

"Are you trying to infuriate me?!" The old man's eyes were wide open. "If you anger me to death, you can go and find a sugar daddy? Don't even think about it, I still have years to live!" "Don't talk nonsense, what are you going to do?" Huniu's heart pounded, but her words were firm. "What am I going to do? Haven't I said it already, either him or me, either me or him! I can't let a stinking rickshaw puller get away with everything!"

Xiangzi threw down the broom, straightened up, looked directly at Liu Si, and asked, "Who are you talking about?" Liu Si burst into wild laughter: "Haha, are you trying to rebel, you brat? I'm talking about you, who else! Get out of here right now! I thought you were decent, I gave you face, but you dare to stir up trouble with me, don't you even know who I am! Get out! Don't let me see you again, coming here to take advantage, huh?"

The old man's voice was too loud, attracting a few rickshaw pullers to watch the commotion. The card players thought Liu Siye was arguing with a rickshaw puller again, and still refused to look up.

Xiangzi didn't have a glib tongue, he had a lot to say, but not a single word came to his lips. He stood there blankly, swallowing hard. "Get out! Get out now! Coming here to take advantage? I was already kicking people out before you were even born, hmph!" The old man was somewhat bluffing just to scare Xiangzi; he didn't hate Xiangzi as much as he hated his daughter, and even in his anger, he still felt that Xiangzi was indeed an honest person.

"Alright, I'm leaving!" Xiangzi had nothing to say, so he had to leave quickly; no matter what, he couldn't win an argument with them.

The rickshaw pullers were originally watching the show, and when they saw Liu Siye scolding Xiangzi, they still remembered the morning's incident and felt quite pleased. But when they heard the old man driving Xiangzi away, they sided with him again—Xiangzi had suffered so much, and now the old man was burning bridges and turning his back on him, they felt it was unfair to Xiangzi. Some rushed over to ask, "What happened, Xiangzi?" Xiangzi shook his head.

"Xiangzi, wait, don't go!" Huniu's heart skipped a beat, and she realized: her plan wasn't very useful, it was better to act fast, she had to quickly grab Xiangzi, lest she lose both the chicken and the eggs! "Our business, we're two grasshoppers tied to one rope, neither of us can run away! You wait, wait for me to explain!" She turned her head and faced the old man: "Let's just say it, I'm already pregnant, with Xiangzi's child! Wherever he goes, I go! Are you going to give me to him? Or are you going to kick both of us out? I'm waiting for your word?"

Huniu hadn't expected things to happen so quickly, bringing out her last resort so early. Liu Siye had even less expected things to come to this pass. But, since things had come to this, he couldn't back down, especially in front of everyone. "You really have the nerve to say that out loud, my old face is burning for you!" He slapped himself. "Bah! How shameless!"

The card players stopped their hands, feeling that something was not quite right, but they were confused and didn't know what was going on, unable to interject; some stood up, others stared blankly at their cards.

With everything out in the open, Huniu felt relieved: "I'm shameless? Don't make me talk about your affairs, what kind of crap haven't you pulled? This is my first time, and it's all your fault: a man should marry when he's old enough, a woman should marry when she's old enough, you're sixty-nine, you've lived in vain! This isn't in front of everyone," she pointed around, "it's best if we clear things up, with clear minds and eyes! With this festive canopy, you might as well hold another event!"

"Me?" Liu Siye's face turned from red to white, bringing out all his bachelor's stubbornness from back in the day: "I'd rather set fire to the canopy than let you use it!" "Good!" Huniu's lips trembled, her voice extremely unpleasant, "If I pack up and leave, how much money will you give me?"

"The money is mine, I'll give it to whoever I want!" The old man felt a little sad hearing his daughter say she was leaving, but to win this argument, he hardened his heart.

"Your money? I've helped you all these years; without me, just think, it would be strange if your money hadn't all gone to wild women, let's be honest!" Her eyes found Xiangzi again, "You tell him!"

Xiangzi stood there stiffly, without a word to say.

[1]

 Dong dong cha, the sound of drums and gongs during a wedding, implying a wedding.

[2]

 Regardless of the seedling, meaning no matter who.

Fifteen

When it came to fighting, Xiangzi couldn't hit an old man, nor could he hit a girl. His strength had nowhere to be used. As for being a scoundrel, he could only think about it, not act it out. As for Huniu, he could have stomped his foot and run away. For this current situation, since she had fallen out with her father and was willing to go with him; no one knew what was in her heart, but on the surface, she was sacrificing for Xiangzi; in front of everyone, he couldn't help but show some heroic spirit. He had nothing to say, he could only stand there and wait for things to become clear; at least he had to do this to be like a man.

The Liu family father and daughter were left staring at each other, with nothing more to say; Xiangzi was silent. The rickshaw pullers, no matter whose side they were on, seemed to find it difficult to interject. The card players couldn't help but speak, the silence had become too awkward. However, everyone could only offer superficial platitudes, advising both sides not to get too angry, to speak slowly, and that no problem was insurmountable. They could only say these things, unable to solve anything, nor did they want to solve anything. Seeing that neither side was willing to compromise, then, even an honest official finds it hard to settle family disputes, so they would slip away when they had the chance.

Before everyone had completely slipped away, Huniu grabbed Mr. Feng from the Tianshun Coal Factory: "Mr. Feng, don't you have space in your shop? Let Xiangzi stay for two days first. Our business will be settled quickly, we can't occupy your space for long. Xiangzi, you go with Mr. Feng, we'll meet tomorrow to discuss our affairs. I'm telling you, when I leave my parents' house, I won't leave this door unless I'm in a sedan chair! Mr. Feng, I'm entrusting him to you, I'll come to you for him tomorrow!"

Mr. Feng gasped, unwilling to take on this responsibility. Xiangzi was eager to leave, and said, "I won't run away!"

Huniu glared at the old man, returned to her room, and began to cry in a high-pitched voice, locking the door from the inside.

Mr. Feng and the others persuaded Liu Siye to go inside as well. The old man brought out his social charm again, asking everyone not to leave and to have a few more drinks: "Everyone, rest assured, from now on, she is she, and I am I, we will never argue again. Let her go, it's as if I never had such a girl. I've been a socialite my whole life, and she has made me lose all face! Twenty years ago, I would have torn both of them apart! Now, let her be; if she expects a single copper coin from me, it's impossible! Not a single cent! Not a single cent! Let's see how she lives! Let her taste it, then she'll know whether her father is better or a wild man! Don't leave, have another drink!" Everyone offered a few perfunctory remarks, eager to avoid trouble.

Xiangzi went to the Tianshun Coal Factory.

Things indeed progressed quickly. Huniu rented two small north-facing rooms in a large courtyard in Maojiawan; she immediately found someone to paper them, making them spotless; she asked Mr. Feng to write a few auspicious characters, which she pasted in the rooms. Once the rooms were ready, she went to order a sedan chair: a "full of stars" sedan chair, with sixteen musicians,"""No golden lamps, no attendants. Everything was arranged, she herself made a red silk wedding dress; she made it before the New Year, so she wouldn't have to start sewing after the fifth day of the New Year. The wedding day was set for the sixth day of the first lunar month, which was both an auspicious day and didn't require avoiding any doors. She arranged all of this herself, telling Xiangzi to buy new clothes from head to toe: "This only happens once in a lifetime!"

Xiangzi only had five yuan in his hand!

Huniu glanced at him again: "What? What about the thirty-odd yuan I gave you?"

Xiangzi had no choice but to tell the truth, telling her all about the Cao residence. She blinked, half-believing, half-doubting: "Alright, I don't have time to argue with you, let's each follow our conscience! Here's fifteen yuan! If you don't dress up like a newlywed by the day, you'd better watch out!"

On the sixth day, Huniu rode in the sedan chair. She didn't exchange a word with her father, had no brothers to escort her, and no congratulations from relatives and friends; only the gongs and drums on the streets after the New Year sounded lively. The sedan chair steadily passed Xi'an Gate and Xisi Pailou, attracting some admiration and emotion from people dressed in new clothes—especially shop assistants.

Xiangzi, wearing new clothes bought from Tianqiao, his face flushed, wore a satin cap that cost thirty cents. He seemed to have forgotten himself, foolishly watching and listening to everything, as if he didn't even recognize himself. He moved from a coal shop into a newly whitewashed room, not knowing what was happening: everything before was like a coal yard, piles of black; now he vaguely entered the new room, dazzlingly white, with several blood-red wedding characters pasted on the walls. He felt a kind of mockery, a white, vague, suffocating feeling. Inside the room, there were Huniu's original table, chairs, and bed; the stove and vegetable counter were new; a feather duster with five-colored chicken feathers was stuck in the corner. He recognized the table and chairs, but felt unfamiliar with the stove, vegetable counter, and feather duster. The old and new objects together made him recall the past and worry about the future. Everything was arranged by others, and he himself was like an old thing, yet also like a new thing, some kind of ornament, some strange object; he didn't recognize himself. He couldn't think of crying, he couldn't think of laughing, his large hands and feet moved in this small, warm room, like a large rabbit in a small wooden cage, its eyes red, looking outside, looking inside, with legs that could run, but nowhere to run! Huniu wore a red jacket, her face powdered white and rouged, her eyes fixed on him. He dared not look at her directly. She was also some strange thing, both old and new, a girl, yet also a woman; like a female, yet also like a male; like a human, yet also like some fierce beast! This beast, wearing a red jacket, had already caught him, and was preparing to thoroughly deal with him. Anyone could deal with him, but this beast was particularly formidable, wanting to stay by his side every moment, glaring at him, smiling at him, and able to hold him tightly, sucking all his strength dry. He couldn't escape. He took off his satin cap, staring blankly at the red knot on the cap until his eyes blurred—he turned his head, and the wall was covered with red dots, swirling, jumping, with a larger, red one in the middle, Huniu with an ugly smile on her face! On the wedding night, Xiangzi finally understood: Huniu was not pregnant. Like a magician, she explained to him: "If I didn't trick you like this, how would you wholeheartedly agree! I stuffed a pillow in my waistband! Haha, haha!" She laughed until tears flowed: "You silly thing! Don't mention it, anyway, I've been fair to you; what kind of person are you, what kind of person am I? I argued with my father and came with you, aren't you grateful?" The next day, Xiangzi went out very early. Most of the shops had already opened for business, but some were still closed. The spring couplets on the doors were still bright red, but some of the yellow paper decorations had been torn by the wind. The street was very quiet, but there were many rickshaws, and the rickshaw pullers seemed more energetic than usual, almost all wearing new shoes, with a piece of red paper pasted on the back of their rickshaws. Xiangzi envied these rickshaw pullers, feeling that they had a bit of a New Year's spirit, while he had been cooped up in a gourd for several days; they were all contentedly getting by, while he had no livelihood, wandering idly on the main street. He was uneasy about being idle, but if he wanted to plan for tomorrow, he had to discuss it with Huniu—his wife; he was begging for food from his wife—such a wife!—He had grown so tall and had so much strength for nothing. First, he had to serve his wife, that red-jacketed, tiger-toothed thing; that blood-sucking thing; he was no longer a human, but just a piece of meat. He had lost himself, struggling in her teeth, like a small mouse caught by a cat. He didn't want to discuss it with her, he had to leave; he had made up his mind to leave without saying goodbye. There was nothing wrong with that, she was a female monster who would play tricks with pillows! He was vexed, he not only wanted to tear up his new clothes, but also wanted to wash himself inside and out in clean water, he felt sticky with something unclean and disgusting, which made him sick at heart. He wished he would never see her again!

Where to go? He had no destination. On weekdays, when pulling a rickshaw, his legs followed others' words, but today, his legs were free, and his heart was lost. He walked south along Xisi Pailou, out of Xuanwu Gate: the road was so straight, and his heart wouldn't turn. Out of the city gate, still heading south, he saw a bathhouse. He decided to take a bath.

Stripped naked, looking at his limbs, he felt extremely ashamed. He got into the pool, the hot water made his whole body feel a bit numb, he closed his eyes, and his body felt tingly, as if radiating some accumulated filth. He almost dared not touch himself, his heart empty, large beads of sweat rolling down his head. Not until his breathing became a little rapid did he lazily climb out, his whole body red, like a newborn baby. He seemed to dare not walk out like that, wrapping himself in a large towel, he still felt ugly; although sweat was dripping down, he still felt unclean—the filth in his heart seemed impossible to wash away forever: in Liu Siye's eyes, in the eyes of everyone who knew him, he would always be a man who stole women!

Before the sweat had completely dried, he hurriedly put on his clothes and ran out. He was afraid of everyone seeing his nakedness! Out of the bathhouse, a cool breeze made him feel light. The street was also much livelier than before. The clear sky gave everyone's faces some radiance. Xiangzi's heart was still aching, not knowing where to go. South, east, then south again, he rushed to Tianqiao. After the New Year, around nine o'clock, the shop apprentices had already finished breakfast and arrived here. Various stalls and performance venues were set up and occupied early. When Xiangzi arrived, there were already circles of people, with gongs and drums playing inside. He had no heart to watch any entertainment, he could no longer smile.

On weekdays, the crosstalk performers, bear trainers, magicians, storytellers, yangge dancers, drum storytellers, and martial artists here could provide him with genuine joy, making him laugh heartily. He was reluctant to leave Beiping, and Tianqiao accounted for half of the reason. Whenever he saw the mat sheds of Tianqiao and the circles of people, he would recall many funny and lovely things. Now he was too lazy to push forward, his share of laughter was gone from Tianqiao. He avoided the crowd and walked towards a quiet place, yet he felt reluctant to leave! No, he couldn't leave this lively and lovely place, he couldn't leave Tianqiao, he couldn't leave Beiping. Leave? There was nowhere to go! He still had to go back and discuss it with her—with her!—He couldn't leave, nor could he be idle, he had to take a step back and think, just as everyone has to take a step back and think when they are at their wit's end. He had endured all kinds of grievances, why be so particular about this one point? He couldn't correct everything in the past, so he might as well just go with the flow.

He stood still, listening to the chaotic human voices and the gongs and drums; watching the people, carriages, and horses coming and going, he suddenly remembered those two small rooms. The sounds in his ears seemed to disappear, the figures before his eyes seemed to vanish, only those two white, warm, small rooms with red wedding characters pasted on them, standing squarely in front of him. Although he had only lived there for one night, they felt very familiar and intimate, and even that woman in the red jacket didn't seem to be someone he could easily abandon. Standing at Tianqiao, he had nothing, was nothing; in those two small rooms, he had everything. Go back, only by going back could there be a solution. Everything for tomorrow was in that small room. Shame, fear of trouble, sadness, were all useless; if he wanted to live, he had to find a place where there was a solution.

He walked back in one breath, entered the house, probably just past eleven o'clock. Huniu had already prepared lunch: steamed buns, braised cabbage with meatballs, a plate of tiger skin jelly, and a plate of pickled radishes. Everything else was already set, only the cabbage was still simmering on the fire, emitting an extremely delicious aroma. She had taken off her red jacket and put on her usual cotton trousers and jacket, but she was wearing a small red velvet flower on her head, with a small golden paper ingot on the flower. Xiangzi glanced at her, she didn't look like a new bride. Her every move was like a seasoned wife, quick, experienced, and with a hint of self-satisfaction. Although she didn't look like a new bride, she still made him feel something new; she cooked, cleaned the house; the aroma and warmth in the house were something he had never experienced before. No matter what she was like, he felt that he had a home. A home always has its lovable aspects. He didn't know what to do.

"Where did you go?" she asked while serving the cabbage. "I went to take a bath," he said, taking off his long gown.

"Ah! Next time you go out, say something! Don't just walk away so carelessly!" He didn't say anything.

"Can you hum? No? I'll teach you!"

He hummed, helpless! He knew he had married a she-devil, but this she-devil could cook, clean the house, scold him, and also help him, making him feel conflicted! He started eating the steamed buns. The food was indeed more delicious and hot than usual; but it didn't taste good, chewing it, he didn't feel the usual pleasure of wolfing down food, he didn't sweat.

After eating, he lay on the kang, his head resting on his palms, his eyes on the ceiling. "Hey! Help me wash the dishes! I'm not anyone's maid!" she called from the outer room.

Very lazily, he stood up, glanced at her, and went over to help. He was usually very diligent, but now he was holding his breath to do things. When he was at the rickshaw depot, he often helped her, but now the more he looked at her, the more he disliked her, he had never hated anyone as much as he hated her, he couldn't say why. He was angry, but he wouldn't express it, keeping it all inside; since he couldn't break up with her, arguing was meaningless. Wandering in the small room, he felt that his entire life was a grievance.

After tidying up, she glanced around, sighed. Then she smiled. "How is it?"

"What?" Xiangzi squatted by the stove, warming his hands; his hands weren't cold, but since he had nowhere to put them, he might as well warm them. These two small rooms indeed felt like a home, but he didn't know where to put his hands and feet.

"Take me out to play? To Baiyun Temple? No, it's too late; how about a stroll on the street?" She wanted to fully enjoy the joy of her new marriage. Although the marriage wasn't ideal, being so unrestrained was good, just right to spend more time with her husband and have a good time for a few days. At her parents' home, she lacked nothing to eat, wear, or spend; she just didn't have a confidant. Now, she wanted to make up for this deficiency, to swagger on the streets, at temple fairs, and play with Xiangzi.

Xiangzi refused to go. First, he felt it was shameful to take his wife everywhere, and second, he thought that a wife acquired in this manner should only be hidden at home; it wasn't something respectable, the less she was seen by others, the better. Also, once they went out, how could they not run into acquaintances? Who among the rickshaw pullers in the western half of the city didn't know Huniu and Xiangzi? He couldn't let everyone gossip behind his back.

"How about we discuss it?" He was still squatting there.

"What's there to discuss?" She came closer, standing by the stove. He took his hands down, placed them on his knees, and stared blankly at the flames. After a long silence, he said, "I can't just sit around like this!"

"A life of hardship!" she laughed. "If you don't pull a rickshaw for a day, you get itchy, right? Look at the old man, he played his whole life, and in his old age, he even opened a rickshaw depot. He doesn't pull rickshaws or exert himself, he makes a living by his wits. You should learn from him, what's the point of pulling rickshaws your whole life? Let's play for a few days first, things aren't just busy these few days, what's the rush? I don't plan to argue with you these two days, but don't deliberately provoke me!"

"Let's discuss it first!" Xiangzi decided not to give in. Since he couldn't just stomp his foot and leave, he had to find a way to do things, he had to take a stand first, not swing back and forth like a swing.

"Alright, you speak!" She pulled over a stool and sat by the stove. "How much money do you have?" he asked.

"Isn't that right? I knew you'd ask about that! You're not marrying a wife, you're marrying that money, right?"

Xiangzi felt choked by a gust of wind, swallowing several times. Old Man Liu, and the rickshaw pullers at the depot, all thought he was greedy for money, which was why he got involved with Huniu; now,"She said it herself! Her own car, her own money, lost for no reason, and now it's buried under her wife's few pennies; he has to swallow his pride just to eat! He wished he could grab her by the neck, squeeze! Squeeze! Squeeze! Until her eyes rolled back! Squeeze the life out of everything, and then slit his own throat. They weren't human, they deserved to die; he wasn't human either, he deserved to die; nobody needed to live!

Xiangzi stood up, wanting to go out again; he shouldn't have come back just now. Seeing the troubled look on Xiangzi's face, she softened a bit: "Alright, I'll tell you. I have about five hundred yuan in total. Including the sedan chair, rent—three months' worth[1], patching the shed, making clothes, buying things, and bringing them to you, it all added up to a little under a hundred, leaving over four hundred. I'm telling you, you don't need to worry. Let's just enjoy ourselves while we can. You, you've been pulling rickshaws and sweating all year, you deserve a few days of fun and looking good; and me, I've been an old maid for so many years, I deserve a few days of enjoyment too. When the money is almost gone, we'll go beg the old man again. As for me, if I hadn't had that big fight with him that day, I would never have left. Now my anger has subsided, and Dad is Dad after all. He only has me as a daughter, and you are someone he likes, so if we humble ourselves and apologize, there probably won't be any insurmountable problems. How convenient is that! He has money, and we're accepting it in a proper and legitimate way, there's nothing unreasonable about it; it's better than you being someone else's beast of burden! In a couple of days, you go first; he might not see you. If he doesn't see you once, go a second time; give him all the face, and he won't be able to resist changing his mind. Then I'll go, and say a few nice things to him, and maybe we can both move back. Once we move back, I guarantee we can hold our heads high, and no one will dare to look down on us; if we keep enduring here, we'll always be a pair of nobodies, don't you agree?"

Xiangzi had never thought of this. Ever since Huniu came to the Cao residence to find him, he had assumed that he would marry her, use her money to buy a rickshaw, and pull it himself. Although using his wife's money wasn't very dignified, given the unspoken nature of their relationship, there was nothing he could do. He hadn't expected Huniu to have such a move. If he pulled a long face, it was indeed an idea, but Xiangzi wasn't that kind of person. Thinking it over, he seemed to understand a little: if he had money, others could steal it from him for free, and he would have nowhere to complain. But when others gave him money, he had no choice but to accept it; after accepting, he could no longer consider himself a person, he had an empty heart, empty strength, and had to be someone else's slave: his wife's plaything, his father-in-law's servant. A person seemed to be nothing at all, just a bird that, if it went out to forage for food, would fall into a net. If it ate someone else's grain, it had to stay quietly in a cage, singing for others, and could be sold at any time!

He refused to go to Liu Siye. With Huniu, it was a flesh-and-blood relationship; with Liu Si, there was no relationship. He had already suffered at her hands, he couldn't go beg her father again! "I don't want to be idle!" He only said that one sentence, to save himself the trouble of talking and arguing.

"A life of toil, is it!" she said, teasingly. "If you don't like being idle, go start a business."
